#2124. West Ham & Ipswich Town plot move for 'unbelievable talent' who Craig Bellamy likes

West Ham and Ipswich Town may have traded divisions at the end of the previous campaign, but the two sides are poised to be locked into the same transfer race for an "unbelievable talent" who Craig Bellamy already has "one eye" on following new developments.

One of Bellamy's fresh call-ups to the Welsh national team is earning admiring transfer glances from West Ham and Ipswich, with the latter bidding to return to the Premier League at the first attempt under the recently-appointed stewardship of Scott Parker after suffering an unprecedented relegation last season.

The Hammers have endured an unsettling reintroduction to life in the Championship for the first time since 2012, having headed into this month's ongoing international break positioned outside of the eight-team play-off picture with just six wins from their opening 15 encounters.

Wholesale changes have been undertaken at the London Stadium in recent times, and with Parker now getting his feet under the table upon returning to the club as Nuno Espirito Santo's replacement, the Championship promotion hopefuls are preparing to rival Ipswich in the race for an acquisition with significant potential long-term upside.

Joe Duncan's star is rising on loan at Southend United from Bristol City, which has led both the Hammers and Ipswich Town to shortlist the shot-stopper on their respective transfer radars ahead of a potential move.

That's according to a report from The Sun, who revealed on Monday afternoon that the two sides have decided to begin tracking Duncan's availability following his shock recent call-up to Craig Bellamy's senior Wales set-up for November's decisive UEFA Nations League double-header against Norway and Portugal.

Duncan, having caught the eye on loan with Southend in the National League so far this season, received the nod from Bellamy and made the matchday squad during the Red Dragons' 3-1 defeat away to Norway over the weekend.

It's now said that Duncan's pre-season displays against West Ham and Ipswich encouraged the pair to take up what is described as "an active long-term interest", which they are both ready to progress forward with in an effort to prize him away from the club.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

Despite having not yet made a senior appearance at Ashton Gate after emerging through the Robins' academy, Duncan has gained plaudits for his displays out on loan with 13 appearances to date as the fifth-tier outfit's number-one shot-stopper under a former teammate of Bellamy's in fellow ex-Hammer Kieron Dyer.

The 20-year-old's growing reputation has only caught the attention of West Ham and Ipswich, who could now deal a blow to the West Country outfit by poaching the young goalkeeper on the cheap ahead of the expiration of his current contract next summer.

Duncan is a highly-rated prospect who the Robins will doubtless intend on retaining amid intensifying interest elsewhere, and under-fire boss Michael Skubala recently talked up the academy product's talent when discussing his first-ever call-up to the Welsh first-team fold.

Having previously gained international experience in Jamaica's youth ranks before committing to Wales, Duncan's recent feat collected praise from Skubala as the 44-year-old lauded the 'keeper's "unbelievable talent" and revealed that a big future at Ashton Gate beckons on the horizon.

Skubala, who may not even be in charge of the Robins for much longer, said of the West Ham and Ipswich target: "What a guy. I met him for a couple of weeks before he went out on loan.

"He’s got an unbelievable talent and I think you can see that.

"He is going to be an important part of this club and he has got an unbelievable future ahead of him if he keeps growing in the right way."

As for Bellamy - a previous teammate of Parker's in East London between 2007 and 2009, of course - made the unexpected decision to name Duncan in his most recent Welsh squad in a bid to solve his nation's long-term goalkeeping crisis.

Duncan, quite naturally, is not in contention to displace number-one choice Karl Darlow just yet, but with the Manchester United back-up now aged 36 ahead of 33-year-old deputy Danny Ward, the Robins prospect was promoted in favour of Everton reserve Tom King as Bellamy looks to lower the age bracket within his goalkeeping department by providing more exposure to young shot-stoppers on the fringes of the side.

Bellamy, helped by his relationship with the aforementioned Dyer at Southend, has been closely tracking Duncan's development, and is keeping "one eye" on exactly that. When quizzed on Duncan's inclusion, the Welsh manager explained: "It's a clear issue at this moment, of course it is, because of the age of our goalkeepers.

"At one stage a long time ago, we had the world's number one goalkeeper [Neville Southall], most incredible goalkeeper.

"But this period now, where's the next one? One of my best friends is the manager at Southend [Kieron Dyer], who gives me big updates. He's been over the moon with him [Duncan].

"When I've seen him, I've been really happy with him.

"The National League will test you, it will test your toughness, there's no protection for goalkeepers, so I think it's a brilliant part of his development.

"The more you see him and you see him live then it makes it clear if we think he's going to be ready in the next couple of years.

"He might not be ready right now but we always have to have one eye on the future."

As mentioned, the pursuing pair could have already received a boost in their respective efforts owing to Duncan's contractual circumstances, which could see the Welsh international depart as early as the conclusion of the current campaign if the Robins fail to successfully negotiate fresh terms.

As such, there's every chance that Duncan could leave on a free and, though his age means the Robins would be entitled to some form of compensation, it would likely pale in comparison to the 'keeper's actual market value.

It may just turn out that his future is decided by what sort of pathway he sees available at Ashton Gate, as with Premier League prospects and an inevitable pay rise sure to be on offer elsewhere, that may be the only real leverage at the disposal of City's key decision-makers.

West Ham fans, is this a deal you would like to see the club complete in the coming months? And are you worried about losing Joe Duncan before long, Bristol City supporters? Is a new contract required ASAP? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

Either way, if Duncan continues to flourish in the National League and maintains such strong praise from those within the game, then West Ham and Ipswich could just end up competing with even more interest.

#2123. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2120. Todd Cantwell drops emotional reaction to Tony Mowbray news at Blackburn Rovers

Against all the pre-season odds, Blackburn Rovers have been making a real go of things in the Championship under Tony Mowbray in 2026-27.

The Lancashire outfit just about staved off the threat of relegation under Michael O'Neill last season, but the experienced manager decided to snub a full-time deal at Ewood Park before returning to his post as Northern Ireland boss, paving the way for Mowbray's second stint in charge of Rovers.

Despite a very quiet summer of transfer activity, in which the Blackburn playing squad was barely strengthened, they find themselves sitting 13th in the Championship after 15 matches, some 11 points ahead of the relegation zone and bitter rivals Burnley, and only three points off the newly-expanded play-off spots.

A huge curveball has emerged though in the lead-up to this Sunday's East Lancashire Derby at Turf Moor, with the shock news coming on Tuesday morning that Mowbray had 'stepped back' from his role as Blackburn boss.

Health issues are the reason for the 62-year-old's decision, and whilst his official statement reads like a goodbye to the job on a permanent basis, it's not entirely clear whether Mowbray still holds the head coach role.

Blackburn's Arni Gudjohnsen continued his excellent run of form with a hat-trIck for Iceland ahead of the derby against Burnley next weekend.

Lancashire Telegraph reporter Elliott Jackson has claimed that Mowbray is only 'stepping back' fron the role for now and there is no managerial vacancy to be filled, with his assistants Mike Dodds and Damien Johnson taking interim charge, whilst fellow journalist Alan Nixon has reported that he's 'stepping down' instead.

What is known for sure though is that it's not the first time Mowbray has had health issues that has impacted his job - he had to step away from his role as Birmingham City manager in 2024 just over a month after taking the job as he needed medical treatment.

That absence lasted until the end of the season, where Blues were relegated under Gary Rowett's management, before Mowbray tendered his resignation, revealing later in 2024 that he had been battling bowel cancer.

Given how well Blackburn had been performing under Mowbray's management, it is an untimely blow for the whole club, especially ahead of such a big game.

And one player in particular who has showcased improvement is Todd Cantwell.

Blackburn's skipper has somewhat divided opinion in his two years at the club, and there were fitness issues last season that kept him out for important games, but Cantwell has steered clear of the treatment table so far in 2026-27 and has been incredibly influential in the middle of the park.

The news of Mowbray's health issues will clearly hit hard for the whole Rovers squad, but Cantwell has led the way with his own tribute for the manager via his Instagram account.

Given the way he's managed to transform Blackburn's squad from what was a relegation-calibre one last season to pushing them up into the mid-table spots in such a short period of time is testament to Mowbray's management and coaching abilities.

Now, it is up to Johnson and Dodds to steady the ship, with a busy period of football coming up, with no international breaks now until the month of March.

With Mowbray only officially 'stepping back', it's likely that Dodds and ex-Rovers midfielder Johnson will be in charge until Mowbray's health improves, or he officially leaves and a new manager is perhaps sought if the duo do not get positive results, and that's going to bring big pressure.

A trip to Burnley for their first game is certainly an acid test, but there's a huge motivation now for Cantwell and co to push even further up the standings given what Mowbray has done for them.

#2118. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2116. Southampton urged to sign 'pricey' Andri Gudjohnsen from Blackburn Rovers

Southampton have been urged to make a move for Blackburn Rovers' Andri Gudjohnsen in the January transfer window, with support for Cyle Larin becoming a clearer need.

Will Lankshear is the Championship's top scorer with 13, while Jack Moylan and Andri Gudjohnsen have 10 in joint-second. Cyle Larin has seven for Southampton thus far but neither Lewis Dobbin or Divin Mubama have shone in his absence.

The Man City loanee has one goal and one assist in his 14 appearances, with Southampton needing more in that area of the pitch for when Larin is out of action. That has become more evident for Tonda Eckert in recent weeks.

Louis Harris is FLW's Southampton fan pundit and, with the January transfer window fast approaching, we asked him to name one player he would absolutely love the club to make a move for, while keeping it realistic but being free to be ambitious as well.

He said: "The January transfer window is always a tricky one, I think. Lots of panic buying and clubs usually looking to over-inflate prices compared to a summer transfer.

"In regards to where we need to strengthen in January, I absolutely think we need to look at our striking options. It's quite clear that Mubama isn't working.

"I can imagine City would want to look at recalling him to get him gametime elsewhere. Dobbin has been handy, playing as a striker, but he can't play as a lone striker. I prefer him as a number 10.

"Larin is Larin. And I don't think he has a 46-game season in the tank. Well, he quite clearly doesn't.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

"So, for me, I'd really like to see us go for Andri Gudjohnsen from Blackburn [Rovers]. It could be a bit of a pricey deal to do but he looks a robust centre-forward.

"He's a really good age. I think he'd offer lots of competition for Larin. When you see his goals, he's been able to score them with real number nine finishes, and poachers goals, and he looks to be a bit of a threat aerially as well.

"Which is something Dobbin doesn't offer when he comes in for Larin. On top of that, he looks to have better movement around the box compared to Mubama. I'd really like to see us go for him.

"But I could imagine Blackburn will ask us to cough up a lot of money, so we will see."

Blackburn are positioned to demand around £15 million for Gudjohnsen in January, pricing out fellow Championship clubs. Having signed a long-term deal running until 2028 with a 12-month club option, Rovers face zero pressure to sell.

The 24-year-old Icelandic striker has been sensational in the 2026/27 campaign under Tony Mowbray, scoring 10 goals in 14 Championship appearances alongside an EFL Cup strike and a recent international hat-trick in the Nations League.

Consequently, promotion rivals like Southampton must rethink their winter recruitment plans. Mid-season deals carry steep premiums, and spending possibly £20 million or more on one forward is financially unfeasible for second-tier sides.

Unless Premier League suitors join the race, Rovers can comfortably retain their talisman for a late play-off push. They may always feel like they need to sell eventually, but January should not be a consideration for Blackburn regarding Gudjohnsen.

Born in London as the son of former Chelsea and Barcelona star Eidur Gudjohnsen, the Blackburn forward developed in Spain’s elite youth systems at both Barcelona and Real Madrid.

Seeking senior football, he moved to Sweden’s IFK Norrkoping before thriving on loan at Danish side Lyngby, where a prolific 15-goal campaign earned him a permanent move to Belgian club KAA Gent in 2024. After a quieter spell in Belgium, he joined Blackburn in August 2025.

How much will Blackburn get for selling Gudjohnsen? Comment below!

His debut season at Ewood Park showed clear promise despite hamstring and ankle injuries cutting it short, yielding seven goals in 25 Championship appearances and a new long-term contract. Now fully fit in 2026/27, he is the division's standout marksman.

Only 24, and with plenty of room to develop further, Gudjohnsen already has 15 international goals. Only five players have more, including his father and Gylfi Sigurdsson. He is well on the way to topping that list given his current trajectory.

#2115. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”

#2114. Leeds vs Man Utd preview: Team news, form, injuries and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Leeds United welcome Manchester United to Elland Road on Sunday, with both sides looking to put a difficult run of results behind them and get their Premier League season on track.

Leeds United suffered a 2-1 defeat away to Arsenal last weekend despite James Justin scoring to put them in front in the 55th minute.

Manchester United also dropped points in the Premier League, drawing 1-1 with Tottenham, before losing 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League away from home.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to find consistency in recent weeks and have come under immense pressure to get in results. The Whites meanwhile will hope that home advantage can make the difference when United face their bitter rivals on Elland Road.

Leeds have recorded one win, two draws and two defeats in their last five matches in all competitions. Their best result came against Newcastle, where they won, but a 6-3 defeat to Chelsea in the Carabao Cup and the loss to Arsenal have left them searching for a response.

Manchester United have also won just once in their last five matches. Their 4-0 Champions League victory over Sabah FK was the only comfortable win, but defeats to Manchester City and Atletico Madrid, alongside draws against Fulham and Tottenham, have raised questions about their form and Michael Carrick.

Leeds are waiting on Dan James, who returned from international duty with a back problem and missed the Arsenal game. Joe Rodon remains sidelined with a hamstring injury and is not expected back in full training until next month. Harry Wilson could return after being an unused substitute last weekend. Mateo Joseph is also among Leeds’ absentees.

Manchester United have their own concerns. Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are doubts after picking up issues against Tottenham and missing Tuesday’s trip to Madrid. Benjamin Sesko remains unavailable with a recurring shin problem, while Patrick Dorgu is expected to be out until late November. Manuel Ugarte is recovering from knee surgery, and Noussair Mazraoui is a doubt with a back problem.

There is better news regarding Matthijs de Ligt, who could return to contention. Leny Yoro is also available for selection despite his Champions League suspension.

The match takes place at Elland Road on Sunday, October 18. Kick-off is scheduled for 2 pm BST (1pm GMT).

UK viewers can watch the game live on Sky Sports Main Event, with coverage beginning at 1 pm BST.

#2113. The Brugge issue and a familiar backstory: Why the Tresoldi deal is becoming off-putting

Recently, it’s felt like AC Milan have been set on signing Nicolo Tresoldi in the winter. It may be the easy option, but is it the right one?

At a time when Milan are doing the PR side of things almost perfectly, Tresoldi feels like another easy win. It seems increasingly likely that Francesco Camarda departs in the winter mercato, so why not replace him with another Milanista?

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Replace the boy who was destined to become king with another who seems further down the career line. He’s already scored a senior goal – or three – at San Siro and he’s got attention from all of Europe’s best clubs. However, if the Rossoneri came knocking, would he be able to say no to his boyhood club?

Well, here is the alternative view: maybe they shouldn’t knock at all.

Now, it’s unpopular to suggest that Tresoldi isn’t the right man, but stick with us here, especially after reports today. If you’ve not seen, Club Brugge have made a statement in the Belgian outlet Het Laatste Nieuws that they won’t be selling him in January.

It’s a ‘club policy’ and even for €60-70 million, he won’t be leaving Belgium. We’ve seen these kinds of statements become pointless in the past and this could become one of them. However, that’s not really the important thing here.

Instead, it’s that Tresoldi has now entered a new bracket. €50m in January is steep, but it’s workable. Ultimately, we’re not in record-breaking territory. Taking that €10-20m step upwards, though, that’s where we enter that territory. There’s also a possibility that more would be needed, too.

It’s a figure that Gerry Cardinale may not be afraid to spend. In fact, reports would have you believe that he really loves Tresoldi and sees him as a perfect addition to a strong frontline roster. However, that’s a lot of money on two strikers in one season.

Then, you’ve also got negotiations with Brugge to deal with. Historically, the Diavolo have found it difficult to close deals with them and with Tresoldi, it’ll be harder than ever, especially after the Ardon Jashari situation.

With the figures being discussed too, you can only imagine how small the margins will be.

Then, you have maybe the most difficult factor. Tresoldi, taking romanticism out of it, has only performed in, with respect, sub-tier leagues. Now, that’s not to take away from his records; scoring-wise, he’s been phenomenal and he’s matched it in the Champions League.

However – and it’s a big however – we’ve already seen that it doesn’t always translate. For example, look at Charles De Ketelaere, but that’s not the real parallel here.

Here, we head to the parallel. In the summer just gone, Milan hired a new Portuguese coach, who initially faced a few questions. After an up-and-down first half of the season, they find themselves in need of a statement January window and stumble upon a boyhood fan of the Rossoneri.

Said fan is banging goals in for fun in a league outside of Europe’s top five, but there’s no need to panic as he’s performing in the Champions League, even against the biggest teams. So, the Rossoneri make the plunge.

Six months later, they try to sell him and a year after that, he’s on loan at Porto, with an €18m buy clause sitting in his deal, unlikely to be touched. Of course, that’s Santiago Gimenez.

There are differences between them, of course, but the formula has already been tried and the results speak for themselves. Trying it again, whilst enticing for the same romantic reasons as Gimenez’s arrival, may not be the answer.

Yes, Gimenez had issues with selection and injuries and eventually, both together. Nevertheless, even without these problems, it was a rushed deal and the Diavolo are going to be the ones paying for that rush next season.

The point here isn’t to discredit the Mexican, but rather suggest that rushing to sign a romantic connection isn’t always the best way to spend big money. It’s about balance, which is why another striker signing like Nicolas Füllkrug wouldn’t be ideal.

Denying that Tresoldi could become a great would be naive, but it would also be as bad to ignore the reasons why the deal is becoming more of a gamble. At €50m, it makes sense, but we’re entering an echelon of deals that requires serious consideration.

In January, Milan would probably have to spend upwards of €70m and that wouldn’t guarantee them Champions League football. Nothing really will offer complete assurances in football; few things – if anything – are guaranteed. Yet, for that fee, it would be expected and you’re still not really addressing the issues within the squad completely.

To do that, Cardinale would have to spend at least another €30-40m to satisfy the fanbase and that’s unreasonable halfway through a season.

So, diversifying the money across the team and then reassessing in the summer may be the best route forward. After all, with this increased price demand, you’re hardly going to be paying more in the summer – and if their demands reach that amount, you’re voicing new questions.

With Markus Krösche also coming in, the January mercato will be one where everything has to be considered carefully and judgements will be made before February 1 rolls around. Spending so heavily on one target may also be an early and unnecessary strike on his record, if it doesn’t work.

For many, Tresoldi made sense in January because he’d be an investment at an investment price. Now, you’re looking at a major budget depletion for a striker who has yet to become a senior international and/or a regular goalscorer in one of Europe’s top five leagues.

The romantic side of it is great; it’s difficult not to fall for that. Financially, though, signing Tresoldi is becoming a tougher sell and that’s where we might have to start listening to the brain rather than the heart.

#2112. Huddersfield Town told to make January transfer move for ‘aggressive’ Barnsley star

Optimism is beginning to build that Huddersfield Town can gain promotion and finally make their return to the Championship.

The Terriers currently sit at the summit of the third tier standings after taking 29 points from their opening 14 league games, while having a game in hand on some of their promotion rivals after not playing last weekend.

It had been a disappointing last couple of weeks in Yorkshire after Huddersfield were held at home in the league by Luton Town to end a run of four successive victories, while they also suffered defeat in the FA Cup at the hands of Exeter City, going down 2-1.

Martin Drury’s men were able to respond in their final group game of the EFL Trophy last week however, putting Liverpool's U21’s to the sword in a 4-0 victory that helped them top Northern Group H.

League action finally returns for Huddersfield this coming weekend, with the Terriers hosting Leicester City in a massive game for both teams towards the top end of the table.

Meanwhile, to solidify their place in the promotion picture after a hectic run of winter fixtures, the January transfer window could be the perfect time to make a statement signing and highlight their intent of returning to the second tier this season.

With that in mind, FLW’s Huddersfield Town fan pundit has backed the Terriers to complete a raid of a fellow Yorkshire club for an ‘aggressive’ star.

Much of Huddersfield Town’s excellent form leading into the festive period has been down to their recruitment in the forward areas, with the Terriers looking so much more potent compared to their previous couple of seasons in League One.

The addition of Derensilli Sanches Fernandes looks inspired at the moment, with the creative Cape Verde midfielder contributing seven goals and six assists from 14 matches, while the signing of frontman Ashley Fletcher has added much-needed experience and allowed Martin Drury to chop and change between him or Joe Taylor leading the line, who himself has 10 goals in 13 league games.

Martin Drury wasn't on the touchline for Huddersfield's recent EFL Trophy win, and the reasoning has sparked a reaction amongst the fanbase

Their quality moving forward and doggedness in their defensive duties has seen them lose just once all season so far in the league, with eight wins and five draws collected from their other 13 outings.

There’s such a feel-good factor at the Accu Stadium at the moment, with Huddersfield fans in awe of the job Drury is doing that could finally see them be back in the Championship setup next season.

Speaking to Football League World, Huddersfield Town fan pundit Graeme Rayner has urged the Terriers to raid fellow Yorkshire outfit Barnsley for winger Reyes Cleary in January, believing the addition of the 22-year-old can help the team be even more dangerous in attack, and that the player has ‘all the attributes’ to help the club achieve a top two spot come the end of the campaign.

When asked who Huddersfield should try and sign in January, Graeme told FLW: “I think it’s a player I’ve mentioned before in the summer, I would love us to take a really good hard look at Reyes Cleary, who plays for Barnsley.

“He’s a left-winger, 22-years-old, was the top assister in League One last year. He was playing in a pretty poor Barnsley side and still managed 15 assists and seven goals in 50 appearances.

“This season, he’s playing in a Barnsley side that are right at the foot of the table, they’re joint-bottom of the league, and he’s scored a couple and assisted one in a team that’s only scored 12 goals, he’s contributed in a way to a quarter of those goals.

“He’s quick, he’s capable, he’s clearly got a good finish on him, he’s aggressive, I think he’s six-foot, he’s a big unit, and got every kind of attribute that we’d want from someone, and what we lack at the moment is a really good option on the left that would give us what Derensili Sanches Fernandes gives us on the right.

Huddersfield Town fans, would you welcome a move for Reyes Cleary in January? Could he be the signing that helps get you over the line for promotion? Drop your thoughts below!

“In our front three we’re quite often playing Fernandes and then a big man, little man combo with an Ashley Fletcher, or Bojan Radulovic, alongside (Joe) Taylor. What this would give us is the option to play Fernandes and someone like Reyes, with Taylor through the middle, and I think we’d be even more dangerous, and he (Cleary) would be the exciting player that could help us get over the line for automatic promotion.

“I think he’s got capabilities and potential to play at a higher level. If he’s doing that well in a pretty abject, woeful side, then he could do really well in a team that are playing on the front foot as much as we are these days.

“He goes hunting for the ball, he closes down defenders, he wins the ball, he’s a good finisher, he’s got all the attributes that we need, and he wouldn’t be hugely expensive, I wouldn’t have thought.

“He’s been at Barnsley for a season and a half, his history is not many games at West Brom where he came through the youth setup I believe, half a season with Hartlepool and then onto Barnsley, so he’s not going to be a massively expensive signing, maybe a million quid would do it? I don’t know.

“So, I say let’s have a look at him. He could be really exciting.”

As another transfer window rolls around, the future of Reyes Cleary hangs in the balance, and Huddersfield Town might be the club to finally take advantage.

Cleary had been tipped to depart Oakwell in the summer, but elected to remain with the Reds, but after a diabolical start to the season that leaves them joint-bottom of the division, Cleary may well want to move on and link up with a promotion-contending outfit to then have a route into playing Championship football next season.

And Huddersfield would seem the perfect place, with the Terriers currently so dangerous in the forward areas and having a line that rips apart opposition defences.

Not to mention that a left-winger is the one area that Huddersfield are missing at the moment despite having such an impressive attack, and you feel if that is addressed, then there is no way of stopping them blowing away most of their opponents and dominating the division for the rest of the campaign.

While Cleary’s numbers have been nowhere near the levels he managed to notch last season with Barnsley, you have to feel that is because he is playing in such a struggling side, and those returns would surely get back to their best when playing in a forward line that is so front-footed and dominant.

Huddersfield’s current setup would be a perfect one for Cleary to come into, with the 22-year-old able to use his pace, power, directness and clinical eye for goal to great effect, and it could be the missing piece that makes Huddersfield a shoo-in for promotion back to the Championship.

If the Terriers were able to pull this deal off in January, then their chances of automatic promotion are bound to considerably increase, and it’s a piece of transfer business that the club must get over the line if they are serious about achieving promotion.

#2109. Chelsea Monitor Jarrod Bowen And Crysencio Summerville

Chelsea’s wide-player search has moved back into focus after the Guardian reported that the club are monitoring West Ham pair Jarrod Bowen and Crysencio Summerville.

The update lands during a busy early market for Xabi Alonso’s incoming rebuild.

The Guardian’s latest transfer round-up states that Chelsea like both West Ham forwards, while an earlier report noted Chelsea and Manchester United had tracked Summerville.

That same earlier update also said Aston Villa, Chelsea and Everton had expressed interest in Bowen.

West Ham pulling away at Stamford Bridge 👀Jarrod Bowen stuns Chelsea early before Crysencio Summerville doubles the lead. pic.twitter.com/Ktwr4pKZGH

Alonso officially begins work on July 1, with Chelsea confirming he has signed a four-year contract to lead the men’s team.

The winger search is not just about adding more attacking names. It is about identifying profiles who can quickly give Chelsea sharper final-third decisions.

Bowen would offer immediate Premier League output, penalty-box timing and a direct threat from the right.

Summerville, meanwhile, fits the younger, explosive one-v-one profile that would sit neatly inside Alonso’s longer-term wing plan.

Read Chelsea has already tracked the separate Summerville angle, but this latest dual link sharpens the picture.

Chelsea appear to be testing two different solutions to the same problem: proven reliability or higher-upside acceleration.

With pre-season closing in, that is exactly the sort of market check Alonso needs before deciding how much of Chelsea’s attacking rebuild should lean on speed, touchline width and more ruthless wide output.

#2108. Jamie Carragher names Man Utd Star who impressed him against Leeds

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

Carragher then turned his attention to Maguire, who he felt played a major role in keeping United in the game when Leeds began to push for a winner.

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.

#2106. Como vs Man Utd Preview: Team news, injuries, form and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

UK viewers can watch the Champions League fixture live on TNT Sports 2 and HBO Max.

#2105. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2104. Wolves sent Marshall Munetsi to France - what happened next changed everything

Bethany Patrick is a football writer based in Manchester, UK.

Her work focuses on the EFL, with particular interest in football governance, supporter culture, and fan experience and access.

Bethany is a director of the award-winning Sheffield Wednesday Women’s Supporters Group, where she works on fan engagement, strategy and campaigns for greater inclusion across the game.

She regularly represents supporters in governance spaces across Europe, including government panels and discussions around football regulation and the supporter's voice.

She has previously worked as a researcher for The Stretford Paddock and is a regular contributor to The Wednesday Week.

Wolves’ £16m signing of Marshall Munetsi had threatened to become an expensive false start.

Having arrived from Reims in February 2025, the Zimbabwe international initially established himself under Vítor Pereira, making 12 Premier League starts during the second half of the campaign.

But his influence diminished following the Portuguese manager’s departure, with Rob Edwards ultimately sanctioning a loan move to Paris FC in January.

It was a decision that offered Munetsi the chance to rediscover his confidence and regular playing time.

Now back at Molineux, he has become an important fixture in Cesar Peixoto’s side.

Munetsi arrived at Wolves from Stade de Reims for a reported £16m in February 2025, becoming the most expensive Zimbabwean footballer in history in the process.

His physical presence and ability to contribute at both ends of the pitch made him an intriguing addition - and he wasted little time making an impression.

He scored his first goal for the club against Everton in March and became a regular under Pereira.

However, the managerial change that followed proved less favourable for the midfielder.

After Edwards took charge of Wolves, Munetsi found opportunities increasingly difficult to come by, making just one start before being allowed to return to France on loan on 16 January 2026.

For a player who had arrived with considerable expectations, it was a disappointing turn of events.

Yet, rather than allowing his confidence to suffer further on the fringes of the squad, the move to Paris offered a valuable opportunity to get his career moving again.

Wolves should try and sign Chelsea's Ryan Kavuma-McQueen, who is also on the radar at Sheffield United due to his performances at Portsmouth.

The change of scenery appeared to suit Munetsi.

He made 15 Ligue 1 appearances for Paris FC during the second half of the season, scoring four goals and reminding Wolves of the qualities that had prompted them to invest so heavily in him.

His performances also ensured that his future remained an interesting talking point heading into the summer.

With Wolves preparing for their first season outside the Premier League in nine years, Munetsi returned to Molineux facing another opportunity to establish himself. Under Peixoto, he has taken it.

The 30-year-old has featured in all 15 of Wolves' Championship fixtures so far, starting 14, and has become a consistent presence in midfield alongside Andre.

His experience and willingness to do the less glamorous work have proved valuable in a division where the demands are considerably different.

Munetsi's resurgence is part of a wider pattern at Molineux, with several players returning from loan spells.

Fer Lopez has arguably been the most prominent, emerging as Wolves' leading Championship goalscorer following his return from Celta Vigo.

Nasser Djiga and Tommy Doyle have also added further options following spells at Rangers and Birmingham City, respectively.

Wolves have responded to the disappointment of relegation with an impressive start to the Championship season, sitting fourth during the November international break and just one point off the summit.

Munetsi believes the returning players have helped change the atmosphere around the club after a difficult period.

"With the difficulties we had last year, it was tough for everybody at the club, but us coming back, we came with a bit of freshness," he explained.

Wolves' decision to send him to France may initially have looked like an admission that the £16m investment had not worked out.

Instead, however, it has helped give them an important player for the challenge ahead.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”

#2102. Malo Gusto’s Kante Lesson Gives Chelsea A £75m Xabi Alonso Decision

Malo Gusto’s World Cup has suddenly become more than a France subplot for Chelsea. It is now a live assessment of whether Xabi Alonso inherits a right-back worth protecting, reshaping or trading at the peak of his market.

Chelsea’s own interview with Gusto this week underlined the human part of that equation. The 23-year-old spoke about learning from N’Golo Kante, calling the former Blue one of the best midfielders France have produced and stressing the value of his example on and off the pitch.

That matters because Kante is not just a nostalgic reference point. He remains the standard Chelsea supporters still use when judging humility, intensity and reliability in a high-pressure dressing room.

For Gusto, those qualities arrive at a decisive moment. Reports around the player’s future claim Chelsea value him at around £75million, with the imminent Marco Palestra deal creating a more crowded full-back picture.

That number looks aggressive, but the logic is clear. Gusto is young, capped by France, under contract, Premier League-tested and capable of playing in several defensive structures.

🚨🚨| NEW: Chelsea are open to selling Malo Gusto this summer, with his agent already in 𝐓𝐀𝐋𝐊𝐒 with Manchester City and Inter. Chelsea will only sell if their valuation is met.{@siphillipssport} pic.twitter.com/vNR2bWud9F

The easiest reading of Gusto’s Kante comments is sentimental. It should not be.

For Alonso, who officially begins work on July 1, the important detail is what those comments say about the player Chelsea have been developing.

Gusto has not always looked completely settled at Stamford Bridge. His best games have carried thrust, speed and recovery power, while his less convincing spells have raised questions about final-ball quality and defensive concentration.

That uncertainty is exactly why the next coach matters.

Alonso’s previous teams have leaned heavily on wide players who can receive under pressure, attack space early and still protect the back line when possession breaks down. Gusto has the athletic base for that job.

The test is whether his decision-making can be refined enough to make him a structural piece rather than a rotation asset.

That is where the Kante reference lands with force. Chelsea do not need Gusto to imitate Kante’s position. They need him to absorb the same professional standard: repeat actions, clean defensive choices, no wasted emotion and total commitment to the collective rhythm.

The Marco Palestra deal changes the right side of Chelsea’s squad immediately. Read Chelsea has already examined how Palestra’s arrival offers a tactical clue over Alonso’s defensive plans, and that pressure is now sharpened by market noise around Gusto.

Chelsea can frame the situation in two ways.

One is profit-led. If a club comes close to the reported £75million level, selling a player signed from Lyon for a much lower fee becomes a powerful PSR lever.

The other is football-led. If Alonso sees Gusto as a high-upside wing-back who fits his model, selling him just as the coaching environment becomes more suitable would carry long-term risk.

The complication is Reece James. The captain’s fitness issues, including his current England hamstring concern, mean Chelsea still need genuine right-sided depth.

Palestra may be part of the future, but Gusto is already part of the present. Chelsea have too often needed emergency solutions in that area to treat proven depth lightly.

That is why Read Chelsea’s recent look at James awaiting hamstring scan results matters in this debate. Right-back remains one of the most talented areas of the squad, but also one of the least straightforward.

This is why Gusto’s France rhythm is more valuable than a simple tournament diary entry. Chelsea are watching a player operate inside an elite international camp, learn from a club legend and handle a public transfer valuation that can change his status overnight.

Alonso’s first decision does not have to be final, but it does have to be clear.

If Gusto stays, he needs a defined role early in pre-season and a pathway that makes sense alongside Palestra and James. If Chelsea sell, they need to do it at a number that justifies weakening one of the few squad areas where youth, speed and top-level experience already overlap.

Gusto’s Kante lesson, then, is not just a feel-good Chelsea connection. It is a reminder of the standard Alonso must demand before deciding whether £75million is a price to accept or a warning sign that Chelsea should hold firm.

#2100. Michael Carrick handed lifeline ahead of Chelsea trip

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2099. Senegal Knockout Tie Gives Chelsea A Double Xabi Alonso Audit

Chelsea’s World Cup watch has moved beyond simple minutes management.

Senegal’s Round-of-32 meeting with Belgium now gives Xabi Alonso a live read on two very different development tracks.

Nicolas Jackson’s attacking authority is the immediate question. Mamadou Sarr’s defensive readiness is the longer-term one.

Chelsea confirmed that Senegal kept their knockout hopes alive with a 5-0 win over Iraq, with Jackson featuring from the bench.

Senegal have since moved into the knockouts, where they face Belgium in Seattle on Wednesday 1 July.

Jackson and Sarr travel into that game with contrasting club questions attached. Both still carry clear Chelsea relevance.

Sarr has not been the loudest Chelsea story of the tournament.

That may actually make this test more useful.

The 20-year-old is still moving through the awkward stage between prospect, squad option and saleable BlueCo asset.

Knockout football against Belgium can sharpen that picture quickly.

The timing is awkward in the best possible way for Chelsea. Alonso steps into a squad with expensive centre-backs, academy-trained options and a board still comfortable using the market aggressively.

A disciplined Sarr performance would not end that search. It would give the recruitment team a stronger internal benchmark before committing another major fee.

Chelsea need defenders who can hold a high line, cover wide channels and still pass through pressure. Belgium should ask those questions more directly than most group-stage opponents.

The context matters. Chelsea’s recall note recorded that Sarr returned from Strasbourg after 18 appearances across Ligue 1 and the Conference League.

The club also noted that he had already earned his first Senegal cap.

That is a different profile from an academy player being carried by reputation alone.

There is a statistical base behind the interest too. When Chelsea completed the deal, beIN SPORTS noted that Sarr made 27 Ligue 1 appearances in his first Strasbourg season.

He helped them keep 10 clean sheets. He also produced a team-high 20 blocks and made 25 interceptions.

Those numbers explain why this Belgium game is not just a sentimental World Cup subplot.

Alonso’s Chelsea defence is already crowded with Levi Colwill, Wesley Fofana, Trevoh Chalobah and external targets still orbiting the plan.

ReadChelsea has already looked at why Colwill’s recovery story gives Alonso a defensive test. Sarr’s value is different.

He is still being evaluated as a physical, long-range defensive project.

Jackson’s role changes the editorial weight of the tie.

Chelsea’s striker picture has been messy for long enough. Any high-pressure international performance now carries wider meaning.

ReadChelsea has already framed the forward’s tournament as a live Senegal striker audit for Chelsea.

Belgium should stress both Chelsea questions at once.

Jackson has to offer penalty-box threat and transition security. Sarr, if trusted, has to defend space against a technically cleaner opponent.

He also has to show whether his recovery pace and frame translate under tournament pressure.

Sarr’s own mentality profile is worth noting. In a Chelsea World Cup Q&A, he named Lionel Messi, Sadio Mane and Paul Pogba as his tournament heroes.

His answer on Pogba focused on leadership and group mentality.

That lands neatly with the exact question Chelsea now need answered. Is there enough presence to go with the physical tools?

This is why Belgium feels like a pathway check rather than a routine World Cup watch item.

Chelsea do not need Sarr to solve the defence in July. They need clearer evidence on whether he belongs in Alonso’s first pre-season group.

He could need another loan. He could also become part of the club’s centre-back market arithmetic.

For Jackson, the question is more immediate. For Sarr, it is more structural.

Together, Senegal’s knockout tie gives Chelsea a rare clean look at two players on opposite ends of the same squad-building argument.

Who can help now, and who is worth protecting for later?

#2098. Barnsley facing January nightmare as two departures predicted

Barnsley are staring down the barrel of a first fourth-tier campaign since the 1978/79 season, and things may be made even worse in January, with EFL content creator and Reds fan Callum Fowler predicting that both Reyes Cleary and Patrick Kelly could be sold.

Daniel Stendel's side were unable to capitalise on some momentum and 12 goals in a couple of cup wins in the week leading up to their trip to fellow bottom-four side Wigan Athletic on Saturday.

A 2-1 defeat at the Brick Community Stadium saw the Tics climb out of relegation danger, leaving the Tykes winless in nine league outings, picking up just two points in that time. They're only off the bottom thanks to a superior goal difference compared to Bromley, who have the same record as them after 13 games.

It's looking pretty bleak at Oakwell right now, and the prospect of losing their best players in January is looking even more likely at this moment.

Despite Barnsley's dire situation, there's no doubt that they currently employ two of the brighter young stars in the division. Both Cleary and Kelly have a ceiling that surpasses the third tier, and perhaps could see them playing top-flight football in their careers.

Cleary burst onto the scene last season with 20 goal involvements in 30 league starts, and while he hasn't been as influential so far this year, his quality is still there, as seen with the cross he delivered for the Reds' equaliser against Wigan on Saturday.

Arguably even more concerning is the potential loss of Kelly, though. Only Derensili Sanches Fernandes and Oliver Norwood have created more open-play chances than the Northern Irish international in League One this season, and he's been the main source of everything good at the club.

Do you think both Reyes Cleary and Patrick Kelly will be sold by Barnsley in January? Let us know in the comments.

Cleary's star power from last season is almost leaving Kelly to operate without the eyes on him, but speaking on the Pyramid podcast, Barnsley fan Callum Fowler and co-host Tom Garratt discussed the possibility of losing them both.

"I just don't see how it turns," Garratt said. "I'll be amazed if you [Barnsley] keep Cleary and Kelly," to which Fowler responded, "We won't; they'll both go, 100%. Cleary will at least; he'll go on New Year's Day.

"Can you imagine that team without Cleary and Kelly? It's basically that team at Wigan with no Cleary, so you don't get the goal, because no one can put that ball in."

If there's one thing that the current regime at Barnsley under chairman Neerav Parekh can attest to doing well, it's ensuring that the club squeezes as much as they can out of buyers when they approach the Tykes for their players.

The likes of Liam Kitching, Mads Andersen, Michal Helik and Carlton Morris have all sold for seven-figure fees since the club came down to League One in 2022, and while undisclosed, it's likely that the late winter sale of Davis Keillo-Dunn to Wrexham eclipsed that, too. Some suspected above £2 million.

If anything, though, that ability to sell well just stokes the frustration surrounding the current hierarchy, as not a lot of that money, if any of it, is reinvested into the playing squad. Barnsley continue to take chances on young players, and it's led to this steady decline down the League One table.

Fowler doesn't think that, if Cleary and/or Kelly are sold this January, they will be replaced adequately, and then a Barnsley squad which is already in trouble will be even worse.

"It's heart over head at times, because I'm thinking, 'surely they'll chuck some money at this in January,' but they never have, not once," he continued.

"They've put the tickets as they have for the Wednesday game [£35 for adults], almost like one last cash grab; they'll sell Cleary and Kelly and bring in nothing."

It's close to universally agreed that Daniel Stendel has Barnsley coached well — at least, better than their league position would suggest — but the general naivety of the squad and the youthful nature often leads them to fade away in games.

The Tykes have led in seven of their 13 games so far this season, but have won just two of those. They've conceded 11 times in the final half hour of games, and four times in the final 10 minutes.

FLW gets AI to rank the friendliest clubs in the second tier this season

The majority of the Barnsley fanbase are still backing Stendel and know that it isn't his, nor the players', fault that they're in this position. A protest is being planned online ahead of their game against Sheffield Wednesday on Sky Sports this Friday, and time will tell what impact that could have.

Plainly put, though, if Barnsley don't strengthen in January, they may be set for the drop. If they lose two of the better creative players in the league, there may be no way back at all.

#2097. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.
