#2096. Michael Olise Gives Chelsea A Hard Xabi Alonso Wide-Player Benchmark

Michael Olise has moved from former Chelsea academy footnote to genuine Ballon d’Or conversation piece.

That should land at Cobham as more than a wistful what-if.

Bavarian Football Works relayed comments from former Chelsea and France winger Florent Malouda, who described the Bayern Munich forward as a live Ballon d’Or contender if France turn their World Cup run into silverware.

The point is not simply that Chelsea once had Olise in the building. It is that his rise provides a hard benchmark for the type of final-third specialist Xabi Alonso must either develop or buy.

Chelsea have spent heavily on attacking upside. Olise’s rise is a reminder that the real separator is not potential, but repeatable elite production.

Bayern’s own season review underlines why the conversation has accelerated.

The German champions confirmed Olise finished 2025/26 with 25 goals and 28 assists from 57 competitive appearances. He was also named Bayern’s Player of the Season for a second straight year, finishing ahead of Harry Kane and Luis Diaz in the fan vote.

That is elite production from a wide creator who does not play like a chalk-on-boots winger.

Olise can receive wide, drift inside, manipulate the half-space and still deliver the last pass at match-winning speed. Chelsea have bought attackers with pieces of that profile, but few have joined the dots with that level of consistency.

There is an obvious recruitment lesson here.

The market will now price that blend of creativity, ball security and goal threat close to superstar territory. If Chelsea want an Alonso attack that controls games rather than simply runs through them, the next winger decision cannot be judged on pace or upside alone.

It has to be judged on game control, final-third calm and repeat output.

The World Cup has sharpened the comparison.

Chelsea’s official France preview noted that Malo Gusto was in the same squad as N’Golo Kante and former Blues academy player Olise. It was a neat reminder of how many Chelsea strands sit inside Didier Deschamps’ group.

Bundesliga’s tournament analysis was even more revealing. It noted that Olise had reached 10 France goal involvements in 18 appearances and was named Player of the Match on his World Cup debut against Senegal.

That same analysis highlighted the tactical adjustment that moved Olise closer to Kylian Mbappe after half-time. His passing range then became the lever that changed the game.

That matters for Chelsea because Alonso’s best Bayer Leverkusen sides were built around players who could receive between lines without killing the tempo. Cole Palmer already gives Chelsea one version of that.

The wider question is whether the squad has another attacker who can bend matches from the opposite side without turning every possession into a one-v-one.

Olise’s Chelsea connection can easily become sentimental. The sharper editorial point is structural.

This is the calibre of player who once passed through the academy pathway and later became a global-level attacker elsewhere. Chelsea cannot change that history, but they can use it to stress-test the next wave of decisions.

That matters for Tyrique George, Kendry Paez, Estevao and any new wide signing.

ReadChelsea has already looked at how Tyrique George’s likely Everton return gives Alonso a fresh winger audit. Olise’s current standing raises the standard of that discussion.

He is no longer just a former academy name or a missed Premier League target. He is evidence of what happens when technical security, role clarity and patience meet elite coaching.

For Alonso, that is the real value of the Olise noise.

Chelsea do not need to turn every academy exit into regret. They do need to recognise the standard now being set by one of Cobham’s former pupils.

The next wide forward who gets serious minutes at Stamford Bridge has to be assessed against that ceiling, not against the safer language of potential.

#2094. Chelsea Could Claim Share of £164m Compensation

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Chelsea fans have followed Manchester City’s financial rules case for years. Everyone at Stamford Bridge knows a guilty verdict could cost City serious money, and much of it could end up with rivals lower down the table.

Reports now suggest an independent panel has reached a decision. If that holds up, the scale of any Chelsea Manchester City compensation payout could run into tens of millions of pounds for the Blues alone.

The Premier League charged Manchester City with 115 alleged breaches of financial regulations in February 2023. That followed a four-year investigation into the club’s accounts. The case has hung over the competition ever since. Every top-flight club knows the outcome could change how recent Premier League seasons are remembered.

This matters directly to Chelsea. If City are found to have broken spending and disclosure rules, clubs who finished below them could argue they lost out on prize money and European qualification. Chelsea have competed directly with City for top four places and cup competitions throughout that period.

According to The Athletic, Manchester City were found guilty on all but one of the 115 charges by the independent panel. The Premier League has not confirmed the verdict or issued a statement.

Manchester City have not accepted any suggestion of guilt. The club released a statement addressing the ongoing process directly.

“The Premier League process remains ongoing, with significant elements to be completed, and subject to strict confidentiality. As such, Manchester City FC’s position remains consistent with the Club’s statement of February 2023. ‘The Club has diligently respected due process for eight years on the basis that the Premier League Board and Executive would behave as an independent, impartial and fair-minded regulator, free from partisan influence.'”

That statement makes clear the club does not see the case as settled. Until the Premier League confirms a result, the reported verdict will linger on for official sentencing.

Data specialists at OLBG have estimated the potential Chelsea Manchester City compensation windfall if City are found guilty. They believe City could owe domestic rivals well over £500m in lost Premier League prize money if the charges are proven.

Within that total, London clubs are said to be due around £164m between them. Chelsea, Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur are among the clubs who could be entitled to a share. Other Premier League sides could benefit too, depending on where they finished compared with City in the seasons in question.

Prize money in the Premier League is tied closely to league position. If City gained points unfairly, that would have pushed a rival down the table and cost them money too. That is the basis for the OLBG figures, and it explains why the sums involved are so large.

For Chelsea, even a share of that £164m pot would be a big boost. It could help fund new signings, or simply ease the pressure around the kind of transfer valuations at Stamford Bridge the club faces every window.

City chairman Khaldoon Al Mubarak has already made clear the case is far from finished. He described the process as having “a long way to run.” That points to appeals and further legal steps, whatever the panel has decided.

Chelsea, for their part, carry on with life under Xabi Alonso regardless of how the case unfolds. Attention stays on squad rotation and Xabi Alonso’s squad management strategy, not on any potential windfall. The financial case will not change anything on the pitch in the short term.

The Premier League has stayed silent on the reported verdict, and confidentiality remains strict around the process, according to reports from Football.London. Chelsea fans should expect a long wait before any compensation, if it comes at all, reaches the club’s accounts.

#2092. Middlesbrough repeating Ibrahim Osman transfer magic can land them £200m windfall

Middlesbrough, unsurprisingly after an incredible summer transfer window, have emerged as serious contenders for automatic promotion this season, but may be looking to the January window to iron out some key areas.

As things stand after 15 games, Middlesbrough sit joint top of the Championship table on 29 points, sandwiched in between Birmingham City on top and Stoke City just beneath.

Despite seeing Hayden Hackney, Morgan Whittaker, and Matt Targett leave the club, Boro enjoyed what is widely regarded as one of the best summer transfer windows in English second-tier history, as the likes of Ashley Phillips, Will Lankshear, Radek Vitek, Sebastian Berhalter, and Amario Cozier-Duberry joined the club.

For all of their strength and depth, Kim Hellberg's side is still far from perfect, and with the January window right around the corner, the Swede could be on the lookout for improvements across the board.

Over the past few years, Boro have recruited incredibly well, and that has been reflected on the pitch by reaching the Championship play-off final last season and now being involved in the automatic promotion race this time around.

Boro have at least one ideal backup in almost every position, but taking a closer look at the final third, there remains an area for improvement, especially on the left flank.

Jeremy Sarmiento's loan move from Brighton and Hove Albion became permanent in the summer, but with just one goal in 12 league appearances thus far, he hasn't quite had the output Hellberg would've been hoping for, as Tommy Conway has deputised off the left on occasion.

With Cozier-Duberry on the right, Lankshear up top, and Riley McGree through the middle, the left flank could do with a bit of an upgrade, and Boro should be looking to get back in contact with the Seagulls ASAP.

Football League World has asked AI to predict the 2027/28 EFL Championship table if Manchester United and Manchester City were in it

Cozier-Duberry was one of the most highly coveted players wanted by Championship clubs in the summer following his excellent year on loan with Bolton Wanderers in League One, but it would be Boro who won the race for his signature, as with three goals and five assists in ten league games already, he has justified his demand.

Brighton have garnered a nous for finding and producing gems in recent years, and have become many second-tier clubs' first port of call when looking for loan talent, as Boro should head to the South Coast once again for Ghanaian winger Ibrahim Osman.

Osman, 21, came through the ranks of Danish outfit FC Nordsjaelland, the same club that has helped to produce the likes of Mohammed Kudus, Caleb Yirenkyi, Mikkel Damsgaard, and Sindre Walle Egeli, and joined Brighton in the summer of 2024 for around £16 million.

Since then, loans to Feyenoord, Auxerre, and Birmingham have followed, but with just over 200 minutes of first-team football across all competitions so far this season, he could do with a loan to the Championship, and Middlesbrough should oblige.

After six months in France during the first half of the 2025/26 campaign, Osman headed to St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park for the second half of the year, and in the Second City, displayed his class.

In 17 Championship appearances for Birmingham, Osman scored two and assisted two, playing predominantly off the left flank where he could cut in on his preferred right foot. Whilst his output wasn't anything other-worldly, his ability on the ball was evident, with brilliant close control and footwork to beat opposition with ease.

Ibrahim Osman's Birmingham City Championship Stats 2025-26

With Boro dominating possession more often than not, Osman will not be required to carry the ball up the pitch too often, but with the ability to take the edge of the game by driving his team forward, he could still certainly be a valuable asset for Hellberg's side.

As another option on the left flank, Osman could be a game-changer for Boro, and a player that could be the difference between automatic promotion and the play-offs, as they should be doing everything they can to lure the Ghanaian to Teesside this winter.

Would you like to see Ibrahim Osman at Middlesbrough? If not, where should he go, and who should Boro sign instead, if anyone? Share your thoughts below!

Though Hellberg will be largely pleased with the way his side looks right now, the January window presents an opportunity to refine it even more, and he mustn't let the opportunity go to waste.

Of course, any deal that Boro strike must be the right one for the club, and one that seriously elevates the level of the squad to help them get across the finish line.

Osman could be just that, and given his lack of minutes for Brighton this season, he would surely relish a return to the second tier, and with Boro, he can go for gold.

#2089. Jamie Gittens suffers Chelsea injury blow with England U21s

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Gittens has withdrawn from the England Under-21 squad. He suffered an ankle injury during Tuesday’s qualifier against Kazakhstan at Molineux. The Chelsea winger left the ground on crutches. He has returned to Stamford Bridge, where the Jamie Gittens injury will now be assessed by the club’s medical staff.

Gittens picked up the knock after a challenge during the second half of England’s win over Kazakhstan. He was withdrawn as a precaution. He did not take any further part in the match. England have been careful not to overstate the issue and no further reports have come out further.

The 21-year-old struggled to put weight on the affected ankle and was seen limping around Molineux after full time. That prompted an immediate release back to his club, rather than any attempt to assess him further in camp.

England Under-21 boss Lee Carsley addressed the injury directly after the game. He confirmed the nature of the knock and the decision to send Gittens home.

“Jamie got a kick on his ankle,” Carsley said after the match. “We never take any risks. With the age of these players, we don’t ever keep them in camp to see how they are. He will go back.”

England’s statement on the squad changes, detailed in a report from Football.London, confirmed the wider fallout from the fixture.

“Mukasa’s call-up follows the withdrawal of Chelsea’s Jamie Gittens and Norwich City pair Kellen Fisher and Ben Chrisene through injury,” the statement read. West Ham midfielder Divine Mukasa has been called up as cover for all three.

Gittens has been an unused substitute in every Premier League fixture Chelsea have played this season, with his only two appearances under Xabi Alonso coming in the Carabao Cup. Those minutes have offered him little platform after his big summer move.

Alonso’s squad selection has been a talking point since he took charge. Our earlier look at Xabi Alonso’s squad choices at Stamford Bridge covered how competitive things have become. An ankle injury will not change his pecking order in the short term. It does, though, remove any chance of him pushing his case this week.

Chelsea’s break has brought other headlines too. Cole Palmer also withdrew, for entirely different reasons, after his recall by senior boss Thomas Tuchel to the full England squad. Chelsea will be watching both returns closely before domestic football resumes.

Chelsea have not yet issued a formal update on the severity of the injury. Any recovery timeline will depend on an assessment by the club’s medical team once Gittens is back at Cobham. Nothing beyond the crutches seen at Molineux has been confirmed.

Given his limited game time this season, the immediate concern is less about missed fixtures than a setback to his integration under Alonso. Fans can follow how the squad shapes up before the next fixtures through our recent line-up updates. Further news on Gittens is expected once Chelsea’s medical staff finish their evaluation.

#2088. “Quite simply disgusting”: Gary Neville tears into Manchester United over Carrick sacking

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Gary Neville has launched a furious attack on Manchester United’s leadership over the way the club handled Michael Carrick’s dismissal.

United confirmed on Thursday that Carrick had been sacked, four days after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left them 18th in the Premier League with eight points from 10 games.

The club said it had reached the “extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary”.

Neville had already branded the process “classless” on The Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast before the announcement, pointing out that it was widely known Carrick was about to go while United were holding talks with potential replacements.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the decision was confirmed, the former United captain went even further.

“It’s just quite simply disgusting,” he said.

“That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you’d be getting sued.”

Neville took particular aim at the decision to hold the crucial meeting in Monaco, with chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flying out to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe while Carrick was still taking training at Carrington.

“The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it’s just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership,” he said.

It seems the only ones not to realise that are the trio above, but they aren’t stupid. How they explain it, or attempt to rationalise, it will be painful to listen to.

Neville also argued the club should have acted sooner. Looking back to the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth in October, he said United “effectively watched Michael Carrick resign on the pitch” as he waved goodbye to supporters at full time, only for the board to hand him three more games.

He summed up the episode as “indecision, lack of authority, just basically really poor leadership”.

It’s more than an episode, it’s an omnishambles that reflects the dysfunctional state of MUFC in its current guise.

United are now expected to move quickly for Mauricio Pochettino. The United States head coach is reported to have reached an agreement in principle with the club, which is prepared to pay his release clause.

First, they will need to wait until the USMNT completes its CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

Yes. Man United to wait until an international break to appoint an international manager…

For Neville, though, the bigger problem lies above the dugout and no manager can be the scapegoat for that massive issue.

#2087. Manu Koné: Roma slap £86m price tag on Chelsea target

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Roma have put an £86m (€100m) price tag on Manu Koné, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, reviving the Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link that first emerged over the summer. Roma want any interested club, including Chelsea, to know the price before serious talks can even begin.

Koné remains under contract at the Stadio Olimpico, and he has become a key part of Roma’s midfield since his move from Borussia Mönchengladbach. The Gazzetta report reads like a warning to potential buyers rather than proof of any talks between the two clubs.

Roma sporting director Tony D’Amico has confirmed Chelsea made contact late in the summer window, though he was quick to draw a clear line under how far things went.

“There was an inquiry and some preliminary contact with Chelsea on the final day of the transfer window. No official offers arrived that would allow us to approach the player. Something was imagined that simply didn’t happen. There was no official offer; the final day of the transfer window got everyone a little worked up.”

D’Amico’s comments confirm Chelsea looked at signing Koné before the deadline, but they stop well short of showing an official bid was ever made. He also explained why Roma had no wish to sell a key player so close to the window closing.

“Before the match against Fiorentina (on 24 August), I said it would be difficult for us to deprive ourselves of important players so near to the end of the transfer session, and so it proved. Kone is an important player, we’re happy he stayed with us, and we’re confident he’ll give his contribution during the season.”

Chelsea’s midfield has changed shape under Xabi Alonso since the summer, with Morgan Rogers arriving to add depth and competition for places. That signing came during a window that also saw exits acceleration under Xabi Alonso, as the head coach reshaped his squad around his own style.

Koné’s ball winning and his range of progressive passing would suit Alonso well, should Chelsea ever revisit their interest. Supporters have already seen how Alonso rotates his midfield for the biggest games, including the recent derby line-up selections against Brentford.

For now, Rogers and Chelsea’s existing central options give Alonso enough cover without needing an immediate signing. Roma’s reluctance to sell also means any deal would need patience rather than a quick resolution.

Nothing in D’Amico’s comments suggests Roma are open to a sale in the near term, and Koné has given no sign that he wants to leave. The £86m valuation, reported by Football London, stands as Roma’s marker rather than an asking price involved with Chelsea.

Will Chelsea return with a more formal approach in a future window? D’Amico’s comments give no hint either way. The Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link is unlikely to fade given Chelsea’s history of scouting France’s leading midfielders, but any fresh move will have to wait for Roma’s stance to soften.

#2086. BREAKING: Mauricio Pochettino agrees Manchester United deal to become new manager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have confirmed an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next head coach, with the Argentine set to take charge after the international break.

The 54-year-old will replace Michael Carrick, who was dismissed on Thursday following United’s disastrous start to the Premier League season. Pochettino is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year contract that will keep him at Old Trafford until the summer of 2029.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is currently managing the United States national team but is expected to fly straight to Manchester after completing his USA international games.

Pochettino will first oversee the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final ties against Haiti on Saturday and next Tuesday. He is expected to arrive in Manchester on Wednesday before taking charge of the club on Thursday.

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Carrick’s departure came less than six months after he signed a two-year contract. His second spell in charge ended after United managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, leaving them in the relegation zone.

Carrick’s coaching staff, including Jonny Evans, Jonathan Woodgate, Steve Holland, Travis Binnion and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson, have all left alongside Carrick.

A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Joao Pedro missed Chelsea’s 3-0 Premier League defeat to Brentford with a knee problem, and the injury has since ruled him out of Brazil’s squad for the friendlies against Australia and India.

He was absent from Xabi Alonso’s confirmed lineups against Brentford, with Chelsea turning to other forward options instead.

Brazil’s coaching staff confirmed the withdrawal, pointing to the same knee issue that kept him out of the derby. Chelsea have not set a firm return date for Joao Pedro, leaving supporters to wait for further updates during the international break.

The Joao Pedro injury situation took a new turn when the Standard reported that he is seeing a specialist during the international break to speed up his recovery.

According to the Standard, the forward is using this pause in the calendar to get ahead of the problem rather than wait for Chelsea to resume domestic action.

Chelsea have not issued their own statement on the specialist visit, and the club’s official line on Joao Pedro’s fitness remains limited to his withdrawal from matchday duty.

Full detail on the reported treatment can be found in football.london’s reported injury update, which first carried the specialist claim alongside Xabi Alonso’s comments on the striker’s season.

It suggests a measured approach, with recovery prioritised over rushing Joao Pedro back into contention for the next fixture.

With Joao Pedro sidelined, Alonso must lean again on the depth built into his squad.

His approach to rotation, part of the squad management this season, faces another test while his in-form forward is unavailable.

#2084. Newcastle United slap massive €80m price tag on defender amid Manchester United interest

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are currently in the Premier League relegation zone after winning just one of their opening 10 league matches.

Their defensive record has been shambolic, and Pochettino needs to fix it as soon as possible. They have conceded 19 goals in the Premier League this season, the third fewest after Crystal Palace and Ipswich Town.

Their defensive struggles could encourage the Old Trafford board to invest in a new centre-back, with Newcastle’s Malick Thiaw emerging as a potential option.

Amid their defensive struggles, Manchester United are among several clubs keeping tabs on Malick Thiaw as they look to address their problems under new manager, Mauricio Pochettino.

Newcastle United have set a massive € 80 million asking price for Malick Thiaw amid growing interest in the German international.

Several top European clubs have the 25-year-old centre-back on their radar after his impressive performances at St James’ Park. However, Newcastle are determined to not lose him on a cheap.

According to reports by Sky Germany, Matthias Jaissle’s side would want at least €80m for Thiaw, who remains under contract with the Magpies until 2029.

The German defender has also earned recognition from national team manager Jurgen Klopp, while Jaissle considers him an important part of his Newcastle side.

Newcastle signed the former Schalke defender from AC Milan in 2025 for a reported €35million.

Since arriving at St James’ Park, Thiaw has established himself as a key figure in the Magpies’ defence, making 64 appearances and contributing five goals and two assists.

His performances have attracted attention from several clubs, but Newcastle are in a strong position given his long-term contract.

Although a departure next summer has not been entirely ruled out, the Magpies are unlikely to entertain any approach that falls short of their €80million valuation.

Manchester United have a clear gap to fill in their defence line. Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez haven’t eased the nerves this season and the Red Devils need a change at the back. Malick Thiaw can be the answer but he isn’t going to be cheap cost United heavy on their pockets.

The big question isn’t the transfer value but whether he is the profile that Mauricio Pochettino needs when he rebuilds Manchester United.

#2083. Pep Guardiola Speaks Out as Man City Found Guilty of 114 Charges

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester City have been found guilty of 114 financial charges, the Premier League has confirmed. The verdict follows an eight-week hearing that opened almost two years ago. It closes one of the most significant disciplinary cases in the competition’s history.

Former head coach Pep Guardiola has already broken his silence. Reaction to the Pep Guardiola Man City charges verdict is spreading fast across the division, with Chelsea among the clubs watching closely.

Guardiola joined Manchester City in 2016 and turned them into the dominant force in English football. He left his post earlier this summer, with the club having already worked through plans to replace Pep Guardiola at City.

His exit means he holds no formal role at the Etihad. Yet he moved quickly to make his loyalty clear once the ruling landed. According to the football.london report that first carried his statement, Guardiola posted directly to his supporters.

“I want every single @ManCity fan to know I am here; more than ever. I am, always have been, always will, be behind my club, my owner, my chairman [Khaldoon Al Mubarak], Ferran [Soriano], the players, staff, Enzo [Maresca] and you, our unique supporters. Let me be clear: we will get through this together. I love you all.”

Manchester City chief executive Ferran Soriano has confirmed that the club intends to appeal the verdict. The statement from Guardiola comes to City’s defence despite him leaving the role this summer. He is still viewed as the man who built this era of success, even though he no longer sits in the dugout at the Etihad.

The Pep Guardiola Man City charges case matters just as much to Chelsea as it does to Manchester City themselves. Chelsea, along with Arsenal and Tottenham, are among the Premier League clubs directly affected by the outcome. Every point City may have gained through breaches has a knock on effect further down the table.

Most top flight clubs including Chelsea are now pushing for City to face relegation as a consequence of the verdict. Some are reported to be weighing up legal action of their own. Guardiola has spoken about Chelsea before, including Guardiola’s previous comments on Chelsea. This latest statement fits a pattern of public backing for Manchester City, despite the criticism now directed at the club.

For Chelsea supporters, the case is not just about points on a table. It touches on the integrity of seasons they competed in directly. That is why so many rivals are refusing to let the matter rest with a guilty verdict alone.

Manchester City’s appeal process is now the next stage in a case that has already run for nearly two years. Ferran Soriano has made clear the club will contest the findings. No date has been confirmed for when that appeal will be heard.

Until then, no points deductions, penalties or changes to past titles have been confirmed by the Premier League. Chelsea and their rivals will be watching the appeal timeline just as closely as they watched the original hearing. The final outcome could still be a long way off.

Bournemouth will block any Alex Scott Chelsea transfer in January. That is according to reports from the Daily Mail and BBC Sport. Both outlets say the Cherries want to keep their young midfielder as they fight to protect their Premier League status this season.

Chelsea have been linked with serious interest in the 22-year-old. Reports suggest the Blues have already tested Bournemouth’s resolve with a bid worth close to £64 million. Bournemouth have given no indication they are willing to negotiate. Scott, the reports say, simply is not for sale this winter.

Reports in the Daily Mail and on BBC Sport state that Bournemouth have no intention of sanctioning a sale this winter. The club’s hierarchy reportedly view Scott as central to their survival plan, wary of leaning on new signings mid-season rather than trusting the squad they already have.

A fee of around £64 million, first reported by Football London, is said to have been offered by Chelsea during recent discussions.

Scott joined Bournemouth from Bristol City in 2023. He has quickly become one of the most sought-after young midfielders in English football. Interest from a club the size of Chelsea shows how far his reputation has grown, though Bournemouth’s stance makes a January move increasingly unlikely.

Scott’s rise has been underlined by his first appearance for England. He came off the bench for a 20-minute cameo as Thomas Tuchel’s side beat Czechia 2-0 in the Nations League. The manager praised him afterwards.

“Alex Scott has maybe one of the best performances in midfield in the Premier League since the start with Bournemouth.”

“Individually he’s one of the outstanding midfielders so he clearly deserves to be with us.”

“He took another step into the right direction. Very impressive and happy that he is on that route. Now he plays in Europe for Bournemouth and deserves to be with us.”

That praise from their former manager will only increase Chelsea’s interest. Scott’s energy and composure suit the identity Xabi Alonso wants to build at Stamford Bridge. The Spaniard has already shown he is willing to rotate his squad, as seen in Xabi Alonso’s squad management decisions this season.

Chelsea travel to face Bournemouth in the Premier League soon. It is a fixture that will give Alonso a close look at a player repeatedly linked with his club. Build-up to that game is already underway, with team news arriving ahead of the Bournemouth clash.

The prospects of an Alex Scott Chelsea transfer happening in January now look slim. Supporters hoping for fresh competition in central midfield may need patience, with Chelsea more likely to revisit their interest when the summer window opens.

News of West Ham’s winter transfer priorities has come from a well-connected journalist with a pretty good track record for Hammers info: Along with it comes a big question mark over what happens to the present first team members who, if proven correct, will find themselves out of line for a starting spot, both competing for one solitary place in Scott Parker’s squad.

Alan Nixon, writing on his patreon page, has revealed the priorities in his article:

Claiming that, contrary to popular belief, the Hammers will not be prioritising a new goal keeper in January, instead revealing that Scott Parker’s top targets are a striker and a left back addition to the squad.

With Taty and Pablo so very poor, the striker option is entirely understandable, whilst a new left back makes sense with Adama Boiro reported to be already unwanted and West Ham, it is said, having sought to cancel his loan deal early.

Mayers and Scarles could compete for one solitary place on Parker’s bench

But it does place academy graduates Ollie Scarles and Ezra Mayers in the invidious position of competing for one seat on the bench if a new first choice defender is signed. Mayers, on current standing, would be the favourite to stay with the out-of-favour Ollie Scarles seemingly destined for a loan or permanent exit once a new player is secured.

I still watch YouTube repeats of the Cup Final of 2006 hoping in vain that Shaka Hislop grows another six inches and stops Steven Gerrard's injury time equaliser. #2078. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2077. The nine major strategic blunders Ineos have made at Man United that prove they cannot be trusted to choose Michael Carrick's replacement

Published: 21:00 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 13:20 EST, 11 November 2026

Michael Carrick looks set to pay for Manchester United’s miserable start to the season with his job this week – but removing him won't fix the club's deep-seated problems.

Carrick took more points than any other Premier League manager between his arrival as Ruben Amorim's interim replacement in January and the end of last season.

He was rewarded with the job on a permanent basis but the Carrick era has quickly unravelled and United entered the November international break 18th in the Premier League, out of the Carabao Cup at the first hurdle and middling at best in the Champions League.

Carrick's dismissal is the latest shameful episode for Ineos, who took control of football decisions in February 2024 when Sir Jim Ratcliffe came on board as a minority owner.

Daily Mail Sport understands CEO Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox still retain Ratcliffe's backing despite being the subject of fan chants calling for their dismissal at recent matches.

Berrada and Wilcox have failed time and time again and yet seem to escape accountability. Here are nine Ineos mistakes that have dragged Manchester United into the unlikeliest of relegation battles – and raise the question of whether they should be trusted to get the next managerial appointment right.

Michael Carrick's dismissal is the latest shameful episode for Ineos, who took control of football decisions in February 2024 when Sir Jim Ratcliffe came on board as a minority owner

Carrick looks set to pay for Manchester United’s miserable start to the season with his job this week – but removing him won't fix the club's deep-seated problems

Erik ten Hag looked to be on thin ice from the moment Ineos walked through the doors at Carrington.

One of his first conversations with the new hierarchy saw the Dutchman offer to put together a plan to overhaul United’s failing data department. The offer was rejected.

‘I offered my knowledge and abilities to Ineos to help them to construct, but they were of the opinion that they already knew everything,’ Ten Hag told The Athletic recently. ‘They were convinced of, say, the philosophy, the strategy, the work methods. But they came from petrochemicals, from Formula 1, from cycling. Top football demands something totally different.’

That summer, Ineos were caught on the hop by the groundswell of support to keep Ten Hag after United beat rivals City in the FA Cup final, when all indications were they had been ready to sack him. Ineos were put in an awkward spot. That said, they still openly flirted with other managers just days after United’s Wembley victory.

Future England boss Thomas Tuchel met with Ratcliffe in Monaco before he dropped out of the race. Gareth Southgate, Mauricio Pochettino, Thomas Frank and Roberto De Zerbi were considered as Ten Hag remained in limbo, all while United navigated the situation without a sporting director or a CEO.

In the end, with tail between legs, an Ineos delegation flew to Ibiza to interrupt Ten Hag’s holiday and agreed to extend his contract by a year, make changes to his coaching staff, as well as make promises for the upcoming transfer market.

Ineos were caught on the hop and kept on Erik ten Hag (right) after United beat rivals City in the FA Cup final, when all indications were they had been ready to sack him

‘They promised me loyalty, they promised me backing, they promised communication, that we get aligned,’ Ten Hag explained. ‘We spoke about the issues. But the promises, they didn’t keep.’

According to Ten Hag, Ineos went back on their word having said they would not sell Scott McTominay, who joined Napoli that summer for £27.5million, and the manager was sacked 116 days after his deal was extended with another £200m splurged in the transfer window.

By the time Ten Hag was finally dismissed, with a compensation package of £10.4m for him and his coaching staff, Ineos had wasted close to a quarter of a billion through indecision.

Ratcliffe relentlessly pursued Dan Ashworth as he sought an elite sporting director to lead the new sporting structure. He believed the Englishman to be ‘clearly one of the top sporting directors in the world’ and ‘a very capable person’.

Ashworth spent five months on gardening leave after United eventually agreed a near-£3m compensation package with Newcastle United to get him, only to be gone after five months in the North West.

Ratcliffe would go on to blame Ashworth for the decision to stick with Ten Hag and there was a sense that Ashworth was not on board with the move to bring Ruben Amorim in. It all culminated in his shock exit after a 3-2 home defeat by Nottingham Forest in which he was escorted through the press room at Old Trafford with his head bowed en route to a meeting where he was sacked.

Ashworth was paraded as a move that showed Ineos could deliver on their ‘best in class’ mantra, only for them to ditch him embarrassingly quickly.

Dan Ashworth (second right) was paraded as a move that showed Ineos could deliver on their ‘best in class’ mantra, only to embarrassingly ditch him less than six months later

Sir Dave Brailsford, one of Ratcliffe’s most trusted lieutenants, was also an unmitigated disaster. He started off as a crucial figure behind the scenes and his ‘marginal gains’ mantra became a key focus for everyone as he led the renovation of the Carrington training base.

Brailsford drove the appointments of Omar Berrada and Dan Ashworth, and was one of the key men behind ‘Mission 21’, United's bid to win a 21st Premier League title, and ‘Mission 1’, to win a first Women’s Super League title. Both fell under the ‘Project 150’ umbrella he was particularly proud of, to win titles in the club’s 150th anniversary in 2028.

Sixteen months after coming in, Brailsford was removed from day-to-day operations and earlier this year he fully separated from the club by coming off the board.

Brailsford ran football operations at a time of mass redundancies, fans being alienated by ownership, a failing team that produced its worst Premier League finish of 15th, and millions wasted in the transfer market on players that quickly depreciated in value.

Ruthless job cuts, something key Ineos figures have long insisted were essential for the health of the football club, totally destroyed morale and have, according to some remaining staff, completely changed the club for the worse.

In July 2024, staff were told of the need to cut 250 jobs, as company credit cards were cancelled, and staff were asked to contribute towards expenses. In February 2025, Ineos announced a further 150-200 jobs would need to be cut all while austerity measures – the scrapping of free lunches among them – escalated.

All of this against the backdrop of the senior executives continuing to be paid handsomely for gross levels of under-performance.

Having arrived as a technical director working under Ashworth, Wilcox soon found himself thrust into the biggest executive job in the Premier League, as director of football at United.

Omar Berrada (right) is a close friend and supporter of Jason Wilcox (left) and trusts him implicitly, as does Ratcliffe, who believes he has the ‘best eyes’ at the club

He pitched up at United having navigated close to nine months in the same role at Championship side Southampton. Prior to that his expertise had been across at Manchester City in youth development.

Berrada is a close friend and supporter of Wilcox and trusts him implicitly, as does Ratcliffe, who believes he has the ‘best eyes’ at the club. Wilcox was one of the driving forces behind the appointment of Amorim and it was he who would go on to clash with the Portuguese at the end, leading to his eventual sacking.

Wilcox is very hands-on, routinely seen close to training sessions, and has travelled with the team on numerous occasions.

‘When I listen to Jason speak, I get what he’s saying,’ Ratcliffe told The Times in March 2025. ‘He’s a warm character, he’s got a sense of humour, he knows what he’s talking about.’

Wilcox has been accused of interfering too much while not establishing the game model Ineos long promised to implement, regardless of who the head coach is. The director of football was called out in a post-match interview by Lisandro Martinez this season and Wilcox has inexplicably remained in the shadows while players and Carrick face up to the club’s failings.

A patchy recruitment record, all while preparing to search for a third permanent manager in less than 12 months, Wilcox has had next to no positive impact on United’s first team since assuming Ashworth’s job.

Hurting from the way they totally miscalculated and mishandled the Ten Hag situation, Berrada and Wilcox pushed to convince Amorim to abandon Sporting Lisbon mid-season – something he did not want to do – and bring his philosophy to Old Trafford.

It was explained that his 3-4-2-1 system would take a long time to embed onto a squad that had been playing 4-2-3-1 but United's decision-makers were undeterred and forked out £11m to get Amorim and his staff in.

It was explained that Ruben Amorim's 3-4-2-1 system would take a long time to embed but United chiefs were undeterred and forked out £11m to get Amorim and his staff in

A further £250m was spent on the squad the next summer but Amorim's standing took another pounding following a League Cup giantkilling at Grimsby Town

Hiring Amorim in isolation wasn’t an error. He represented a bright young manager that had a clear philosophy – something Ineos haven’t shown in two and a half years – but he had been overlooked by West Ham and Liverpool.

One well-placed source told Daily Mail Sport that Liverpool compiled a dossier on all the reasons Amorim would not be able to succeed at Anfield, largely down to how many players would need to be recruited to make his vision a successful reality.

Amorim was ‘rewarded’ with just two arrivals in January 2025 in the shape of Patrick Dorgu and Ayden Heaven, while the following summer saw a clash of ideas in terms of transfer targets. Senne Lammens arrived despite Amorim seeking a veteran goalkeeper to replace the hapless Andre Onana.

Amorim guided United to their lowest Premier League finish and lost the Europa League final in Bilbao to Ange Postecoglou’s Tottenham Hotspur. A further £250m was spent on the squad with the attack overhauled but his standing took another pounding following a League Cup giantkilling at Grimsby Town.

Relations between Amorim and Wilcox deteriorated severely and they clashed in a meeting on the eve of the trip to Leeds United, which would prove his final game. Amorim unleashed a fierce rant which intimated that figures such as Wilcox and director of recruitment Christopher Vivell were overstepping the mark with their input and that they needed to allow him to be the ‘manager’ of the club.

Having come under pressure to change formation and the style of play, Amorim was sacked the morning after drawing at Leeds.

Rather than actually devise a plan to deliver Amorim’s vision, Ineos instead tried to get clever and turned to Harry Marra, a renowned track and field coach, to come in as a consultant.

He was given the remit of assessing individual player running mechanics, acceleration and deceleration. Mason Mount was one of those who Marra identified and he attempted to adjust the 36-cap England midfielder’s running gait.

Ratcliffe imparted advice on stretching techniques he had learned from running the London Marathon to figures in the Carrington physio room

Add from the episode in which Ratcliffe imparted advice on stretching techniques he had learned from running the London Marathon to figures in the Carrington physio room, you got the picture of an ownership that didn’t seem to understand that top-level football isn’t track, field, or cycling.

Performance on the pitch only got worse after this, not better.

8. Prioritising new stadium over a new left back

Speaking to reporters in Dublin this summer shortly after being given the United job full-time, Carrick used the word ‘push’ almost a dozen times when referencing what the club still had to do in the transfer market.

But Ineos, again believing they are the smartest people in the room, ignored those pleas and stuck to their plan to ‘fix’ one part of the team per summer. It was the attack in 2025, and in 2026 it would be the midfield, with the defence to come next year.

So, despite knowing full well that expecting another injury-free campaign from Luke Shaw was an enormous gamble, they did it anyway and made no defensive reinforcements. It hasn’t taken long for that move to backfire spectacularly.

Instead, the decision was made to purchase land to the tune of £60m so they can plough on with plans for a swanky new 100,000-seat 'Wembley of the North' stadium that many fans believe will never be built.

Rather than fixing the team's defence, United chiefs spent £60m on a slither of land to progress with their plans for a 100,000-seat 'Wembley of the North'

The way Carrick has been treated in this whole saga has been cruel and a sign of why managers should think twice about taking a job under Ineos

An emergency meeting after a 3-0 defeat by Bournemouth last month looked set to be the end for Carrick until Berrada, Wilcox, Ratcliffe and the Glazers surprised the football world and simply did nothing. Everyone just continued with business as usual with no backing for the beleaguered Carrick.

Defeat at Chelsea, coupled with two frantic displays to come from 2-0 down against both Roma and Aston Villa followed. Then came the revelation on Monday that Berrada and Wilcox had settled on a recommendation to the board that they should sack Carrick. But instead of being sanctioned immediately, Carrick spent seven hours at Carrington on Tuesday to take training and watch an Under 17s match.

We are told yet again that Carrick's future will finally be decided on Wednesday at a meeting in Monaco, but in the meantime he must carry on until he gets wind he has been officially sacked.

The way Carrick has been treated in this whole saga has been cruel and a sign of just why managers should – and will – think twice about taking a job under Ineos.

#2076. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2075. Championship data highlights exactly what West Ham’s midfield is still missing

A fascinating piece of Championship analysis from Loft Insights on X may provide an uncomfortable reminder of where West Ham’s midfield falls short.

The graphic plots central midfielders based on two key metrics: line-breaking passes per 90 minutes and ball-winning actions per 90 minutes. Put simply, it measures which players are able to progress the ball through opposition lines while also contributing defensively.

The most interesting takeaway for me was not necessarily which players stand out, but where West Ham’s own midfield profiles sit on the chart.

The Championship’s elite midfielders increasingly occupy the top-right quadrant, combining progression and defensive intensity. That is why the chart identifies Norwich City’s Kenny McLean as one of the Championship’s most complete midfielders, posting outstanding line-breaking numbers while maintaining strong defensive output, despite only featuring for ten games out of fifteen due to an ankle injury.

Meanwhile, Portsmouth’s Ebou Adams is highlighted as a “defensive monster”, producing exceptional ball-winning statistics while still offering significant progression. Sheffield United’s Kalvin Phillips (yes the same player that flopped on loan at West Ham!), Queens Park Rangers’ Jonathan Varane and Norwich’s Jacob Wright also occupy impressive positions, demonstrating the ability to impact both sides of the game.

Mohamadou Kanté is viewed as a dynamic ball-carrying midfielder with strong physical attributes, but his game is built more around energy and transition play than elite progression metrics. Arne Engels offers technical quality and creativity, yet is generally more comfortable operating higher up the pitch than acting as a defensive controller.

The data is only based on players who have played an average of 540 mins so one omission is Tomas Souček, whose strengths have always been obvious. The Czech international on paper would arguably be the Championships most effective midfield goalscorer (3 in 7 appearances so far) and I’d expect him to feature highly for aerial presence. However, if plotted on a graph measuring line-breaking passes and ball-winning volume, he would likely owe more to his physicality and attacking threat than his ability to dictate possession through midfield.

That perhaps explains why West Ham can sometimes appear disconnected between defence and attack.

Too often there is a reliance on direct balls into attacking areas rather than building through the centre of the pitch. Equally, when possession is lost, the midfield does not always possess the combination of mobility and defensive output needed to regain control quickly.

West Ham arguably have midfielders who can create and win duels. What they appear to lack is a genuine “two-way” midfielder capable of consistently ranking highly in both categories.

That was precisely what Declan Rice offered during his time at the club. His ability to recover possession and immediately progress the ball remains incredibly difficult to replace.

The reality is Rice was never truly replaced, although finding a like-for-like successor was always going to be a near-impossible task

Former West Ham midfielder James Ward-Prowse’s position on the chart is also interesting. A casualty of Nuno’s reign, he ranks among the highest players for line-breaking passes but has one of the lowest ball-winning outputs. Ward-Prowse excels at progressing possession but offers relatively little defensive contribution compared to the league’s most complete midfielders.

Above all the data serves as a reminder that Koppen’s recruitment and Parker’s existing choices for midfield should focus less on individual statistics and more on complete profiles.

Because the numbers suggest that is where Championship matches are increasingly won.

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

I think there is nothing wrong with the Midfield. There is just a problem with the short pass mentality. The day of playing short passes across midfield are over in English football. You need decent long passes with good trapping and decent positive movement. How many of West Ham’s passes are square ball or back passes ? Far to many. Particularly back passes are all to often caught on to giving away needless goals. Walker-Peters is a really good example. Having moved forward beating someone, he then passes back in midfield, or worst still carries the ball backwards towards midfield or defence. Play positively, avoid back passes, learn to pass well and trap well and you have a very good midfield. Orford, Kante and other young players are far better at doing this, than the older players.

#2074. Arsenal could use Berta connection to explore Endrick transfer

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal may be able to pursue a January move for Endrick. Andrea Berta providing a useful contact for the forward’s representatives.

A move to the Premier League is being linked with the young Real Madrid player after he failed to play regularly for his current Spanish club. As such, his representatives want to find a new club for him in the winter window.

With Bukayo Saka injured, Arsenal may be looking to strengthen their attacking options. Endrick’s ability to play in central areas and wide areas can make him an attractive option for Mikel Arteta.

But it’s not that simple. The North Londoners are not currently amongst the clubs who are pushing hardest to sign the Brazilian.

The TalkSPORT report that Berta’s links to Endrick’s representatives could provide Arsenal with an ideal starting point if they wish to pursue a deal.

The forward has been offered to a number of clubs in the Premier League as his camp seek to resolve his lack of opportunities in Real Madrid.

Arsenal are known to have been interested in the forward in the past. Newcastle United, Aston Villa and Tottenham are the clubs currently gaining more concrete attention.

It doesn’t mean they can’t make a move to north London. Berta’s connections may help them understand what is needed to get the player to the Emirates.

However, Real Madrid may be more reluctant to let the player go. The Spanish club have been reluctant to allow Endrick to leave before, making a loan look more of a realistic option.

Endrick arrived at Real Madrid with an incredible reputation having made his breakthrough at Palmeiras. However, due to there being competition for places, he has struggled to play much in Spain.

If he were to go to the Premier League, he would be able to play regularly to help his development. Playing under Arteta and competing for major trophies at Arsenal would be a great opportunity.

However, Arsenal would have to consider whether he would actually play enough. Moving to another team with competition for attacking spots may not be the best move for his development.

Arsenal need to decide whether Endrick would fill a real gap in their attacking line-up or simply add to their talented front line.

Although Saka’s injury makes attacking additions more important, they can’t make a decision based purely on the injury. They need to look at how he would fit into their future plans.

Thanks to Berta’s link, Arsenal can look into the possibility of signing him. Whether he is a serious target will depend on Real Madrid’s willingness to let him go and what Arsenal can offer him.

#2073. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

#2072. Arsenal receive positive Piero Hincapie injury update ahead of return

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Piero Hincapie has provided some good news for an Arsenal team that have struggled at the start of the season by confirming that he is now fully fit.

The defender has had two separate injury issues since joining Mikel Arteta’s squad but feels he is now over the setbacks and ready to play regularly.

Hincapie is playing for Ecuador in their November international fixtures. His inclusion in the squad was also a positive, as he was unable to play in their last camp due to injury.

Hincapie initially missed Ecuador’s September squad because of a groin problem. He had been named among the substitutes for Arsenal’s final match before that break, against Brighton.

After recovering, the 24-year-old returned to action in the 2-1 Premier League win over Leeds United on October 10. However, he suffered another setback during the warm-down after the match.

The ankle injury was particularly unfortunate, with Hincapie having to leave the pitch with assistance from goalkeeper Illan Meslier. He subsequently missed Arsenal’s matches against Lille and Nottingham Forest.

Mikel Arteta had at first suggested that Hincapie could be out for a matter of weeks, but the defender has since rejoined individual training.

He has now given a much more positive update on his injury whilst on international duty. He is now “100% fit” and does not have any niggles.

This is great news for Arteta. He has had to be cautious with his defensive choices throughout the campaign.

Hincapie will have a chance to get some much-needed game time while he is included in the squad to play Ecuador.

He has been included in the squad by Marcelo Gallardo to face Costa Rica (13th November) and Bolivia (17th November) in friendly games. This will be the first games Ecuador play at home under their new head coach.

Hincapie has also expressed his optimism for his initial discussions with Gallardo. The player is excited to work under the former River Plate manager and help the national team.

He should prove to be a valuable asset in the squad, considering he can play in both roles. He is a natural centre back but can also play as a left back.

Hincapie has shown that he is happy to play anywhere in the team his manager requires. This can be an important factor for Arsenal as the fixture list gets tougher.

He has only featured three times in all competitions this season and has yet to play in the Premier League. Consistent playing time will be key if he is to become a regular in Arteta’s plans.

However, his latest update shows that he is no longer struggling with the physical issues. If he can stay fit over the international break, he will provide another key defensive option for Arsenal in the coming weeks.

There will be no live UK television coverage of Chelsea’s return to Premier League action against Bournemouth on Saturday afternoon.

Xabi Alonso’s side host the Cherries at Stamford Bridge in a traditional 3pm kick-off, with the fixture falling within the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

That means supporters unable to attend will need to follow the match through other channels, rather than watching it live on Sky Sports or TNT Sports.

Although the game will not be televised live in the UK, supporters can follow developments through the official Chelsea website and the Premier League website.

The Saturday 3pm blackout prevents live domestic television broadcasts during the restricted period, although overseas coverage may be available through broadcasters holding the relevant rights.

Highlights will be available after the match through the usual broadcast and digital channels, subject to their respective release schedules.

Chelsea will then turn their attention to a trip to Everton on Saturday, 17 October, with that fixture scheduled for a 12.30pm kick-off at Hill Dickinson Stadium.

For Alonso, the Bournemouth match provides the immediate opportunity to resume Chelsea’s domestic campaign following the international break.

#2069. Arsenal receive mixed verdict after Liverpool penalty review

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

We’ve an important update on the Arsenal Liverpool penalty decision. Arsenal received a mixed reaction from the Premier League’s independent panel after their controversial win at Anfield.

The panel were unanimous in their decision that Liverpool should have been awarded a penalty after Jurrien Timber tugged Alexander Isak’s shirt. However, they were divided on whether it should have been reviewed by VAR.

The panel also cleared the officials on the incident involving Noni Madueke. This means that Arsenal were justified in their win due to the wrong decision being made on the penalty.

The incident happened during the 1-0 win for Arsenal at Anfield on November 1. Isak was trying to run onto a cross from Bradley Barcola after Kai Havertz had given the Gunners the lead.

As Timber pulled him back, he was unable to cleanly head the ball. The referee Andy Madley allowed play to continue. The decision was then made by VAR Stuart Attwell that there was not enough evidence to suggest a review.

The five-man Key Match Incidents panel has now ruled that this was the wrong decision. Four of the panel voted that Liverpool should have been awarded the spot kick after Timber’s handball.

However, there was a tight vote on whether VAR should have intervened. Three panellists felt that Attwell should have shown Madley to the pitchside monitor, whilst two felt it did not reach the threshold.

One panellist felt the length of the hold was not long enough to affect the movement of Isak. The other two members who felt that VAR should not have intervened also said that it is subjective as to how much the hold would affect the striker.

This is an important point to note. Whilst they agreed that the penalty was deserved, there was less agreement on whether it was VAR’s responsibility to overturn the decision.

There was also good news for Arsenal in the panel’s decision regarding Madueke’s late altercation with Alexis MacAllister.

The Liverpool midfielder fell to the floor following a headbutt from the Arsenal winger. The incident was reviewed by the VAR, but Madueke was spared a red card. Mac Allister received a yellow.

All five panel members agreed that Madueke should not have been sent off. They felt that there had not been any contact, and Mac Allister’s reaction was certainly excessive.

It certainly gives us an alternative standpoint into the situation. Whilst Arsenal benefited from the incorrect decision regarding the penalty, the officials were backed up with the disciplinary incident.

This won’t have any impact on the result of the Anfield game, as Havertz’s goal was the winning goal and gave Arsenal their first league victory at the stadium in 14 years.

However, it does show how risky Timber was to make the challenge and how close the panel were with whether they should have intervened with VAR.

David Martin’s West Ham career was short but never-to-be-forgotten

Martin has been quick to praise the talent available to him in his new role: Speaking to West Ham’s web site, the 40 year old confirmed his own methodical approach:

“I enjoy having a plan, making the very most of myself and my abilities, being a good person and making people around me better…

“That’s what the role is – creating a competitive environment where people can learn and improve under pressure. I’ve got two very good young goalkeepers in the development squad – Fin Hooper and Lanre Awesu – and it’s an unbelievable competition between them and I want that to make them excel and get better.”

Of course with former academy graduate Finlay Herrick now officially Scott Parker’s third choice keeper, (and not forgetting Mason Terry who is out on loan) the Hammers do have an embarrassment of riches in their ‘up and coming’ goal keeping department.

It can only be a matter of time before Herrick progresses – something tells me this might be sooner than we think – and it is good to see that West Ham are embracing their heritage with an appointment like Martin’s to a pivotal post within the club.

Bournemouth’s determination to keep Alex Scott has done little to discourage Chelsea, who remain among the leading contenders to sign the England midfielder.

Bournemouth, however, have no intention of allowing Scott to leave in January. talkSPORT reported this week that the south coast club remain determined to retain him, despite his reluctance to sign a contract extension.

Scott’s current deal runs until 2028, leaving Bournemouth with time to negotiate, although their position could become more complicated if no agreement is reached before next summer.

The midfielder’s immediate priority is recovering from a thigh injury sustained while representing England. Chelsea are not alone in their admiration. Arsenal and Liverpool have also been linked with Scott, whose performances have attracted attention beyond the Premier League.

Suggestions circulating on social media that Bournemouth have already agreed to let him leave in January remain unsubstantiated. There has been no reliable confirmation of such an arrangement, and the club’s reported position is firmly against a winter sale.

For Chelsea, the more realistic opportunity may come next summer, particularly if Scott continues to resist signing a new contract.
