Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have made contact with Bournemouth over a potential move for Alex Scott. Mikel Arteta may have to rethink his transfer plans if competition for the midfielder intensifies.

According to The i Paper, Arsenal, Liverpool and Chelsea have all made exploratory enquiries about the 23-year-old. The clubs want to establish what it would take to persuade Bournemouth to sell. However, none appears close to agreeing to a deal.

Arsenal are believed to be keen on a summer move. Liverpool and Chelsea are more interested in signing Scott in January. However, if they were to make a serious offer, it could be hard to hold out.

Scott has become one of the most exciting midfielders in the Premier League. He is an attractive option for clubs looking to improve their squad going forward due to his age, experience and being a homegrown player.

However, there is some uncertainty around Scott’s future. The England international has turned down two contract offers and is unwilling to sign a new deal. There are 18 months left on Scott’s contract, meaning it is an increasingly important decision for Bournemouth.

Arsenal are interested in Scott with doubts surrounding their own midfield options. There have been reports linking Martin Zubimendi and Mikel Merino to exits.

Scott would give Arteta another option, but Arsenal will have to consider if they want to make a move and give Liverpool and Chelsea an advantage.

Bournemouth are reported to want more than £100m to let him go during the January transfer window.

It would be a big asking price, especially given that Scott has 18 months left on his contract. There are a few reasons why Bournemouth could be holding out for a big fee.

The Cherries had to pay £25m to bring Scott in from Bristol City, who will receive 20 per cent of any future transfer fee. They will also have to find a suitable replacement at the halfway stage.

Reports said Chelsea received a number of offers worth around £70m in the summer, but Bournemouth have rejected claims that a bid of £80m will get them talking.

Scott is believed to be happy to stay at the club until the end of the campaign. Bournemouth are also having a great season under Marco Rose, currently sitting in seventh place in the Premier League with eight straight wins in all competitions.

Arsenal have little to hurry into any deal and could use the summer to negotiate a better deal. This is especially true with Scott’s contract situation becoming more of an issue.

The problem is that Liverpool and Chelsea are keen competitors and could lead to a bidding war in January, leaving Arsenal with little room to negotiate.

Whilst Arsenal have made their interest known, this doesn’t necessarily mean he will be moving anytime soon. If Scott is a genuine target, then they must be prepared to pounce if the situation arises.

The player’s age is another consideration. Conceicao cannot complete an international transfer to England until he turns 18, leaving interested clubs facing a potentially lengthy wait before he can join their squads.

Chelsea have already demonstrated their willingness to agree deals for young players well before they are eligible to arrive, with Estevao providing a particularly relevant example.

No formal bid or agreement has been reported for Conceicao. Nevertheless, the latest claims suggest Chelsea are prepared to explore another long-term investment in Brazilian talent rather than wait until the player becomes available for an immediate move.

Viktor Gyökeres has put to bed any doubts over his future at Arsenal by stating his desire to stay.

The striker has been under question over his long-term future under Mikel Arteta after a difficult start to the 2026/27 season. His latest comments suggest he will not be seeking a move away from north London.

Gyökeres moved to Arsenal for £63.5m from Sporting Lisbon last summer. He’s played a key role in the club winning the Premier League last season with 14 league goals.

However, he has not featured as much this season. Kai Havertz often being Arteta’s go-to man centrally, resulting in him only starting once in the Premier League.

This has led to some speculation that the Sweden international may look at his options in January. However, his latest interview with the Swedish outlet Fotbollskanalen suggests otherwise.

He explained that they regularly discuss football, the team’s approach and the opposition. Those conversations also help him understand what Arteta expects when he is selected to start.

It is a key point to note that just because he hasn’t had much time on the pitch doesn’t mean there have been any issues between the two.

They will also want to avoid losing someone they have invested heavily in so early on. Gyökeres has a contract until 2030, so they are in a great position.

His latest comments will help to reassure fans that he is not looking to leave the club after stating his ambition to play more games and make himself an indispensable part of Arteta’s squad.

The main thing for Arsenal will be to make sure that he can turn this attitude into consistent performances. Gyökeres has made his intentions clear and now needs to show why they brought him to North London in the first place.

Alonso’s attention will now turn to Saturday’s Premier League meeting with Bournemouth at Stamford Bridge.

The match kicks off at 3pm and will not be televised live in the United Kingdom because of the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

Scott’s injury is expected to deprive Bournemouth of an important midfield option, although Chelsea will have their own fitness decisions to make as players return from international duty.

#2061. GdS: January arrivals and a return to the top – Cardinale’s strategy to land van Dijk

Gerry Cardinale wants to return AC Milan to the top and he hopes that adding another elite player next summer will help to achieve that goal.

With the signing of Luka Modric, and the subsequent quality that he’s shown since, Milan may tap into the experienced free agent market more. Of course, opportunities have to be right, but Cardinale is becoming more in favour of signing these types of players.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

So, in the summer, they may look to Anfield instead of the Bernabeu, where Virgil van Dijk could become the new Luka Modric.

Calciomercato.com relays words from Gazzetta dello Sport, building on their report this morning of the interest in van Dijk. The move, it seems, would be pushed for by Cardinale and the American is already working up a plan.

In January, the Rossoneri will strengthen the defence, with Goncalo Inacio and Massire Sylla the favoured names currently. With these additions, the American would hope for a return to the Champions League, which would be essential to signing van Dijk.

So, with the mix of promising youth recruited in January, they would hope to finalise the defensive transformation with a world-class player next season. Cardinale remains convinced of van Dijk’s quality, and he can discuss a move as early as February 1.

With no sign of the stalemate at Anfield ending, the American – who holds a stake in Liverpool’s ownership – remains hopeful.

#2060. Jussi Jääskeläinen praises Arsenal star David Raya

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Former Premier League goalkeeper Jussi Jääskeläinen thinks David Raya is the best goalkeeper in the division right now.

But the former Bolton Wanderers star feels the Arsenal No.1 has more to prove before he can be put up with the all-time greats of the competition.

Jääskeläinen recently spoke to Kasinopartio.com, where he praised Raya’s performances and the understanding he has developed with Arsenal’s defenders.

“Raya has been a fantastic signing. He has a great relationship with the centre-backs; even when the back four changes, everything works well.”

The Spaniard has played a key role for Mikel Arteta since his arrival from Brentford. His organisation of the backline, key saves and contribution to Arsenal’s build up play have helped him to be a standout player.

In 2012, Raya began his journey in English football when he moved to Blackburn Rovers from Spain. At that time, he was just a young teenager unfamiliar with English football.

His short spell at Southport introduced him to senior football. Soon, he returned to Blackburn and forged a career ahead of moving to Brentford in 2019. After four years with Brentford, Raya achieved success at the club, helping them to the Premier League.

The player’s performances in the higher leagues caught the attention of Arsenal, and Arteta signed him on loan to the Emirates in 2023 before making the move permanent the following summer.

It didn’t take long for Raya to establish himself in the team. He took home the Premier League Golden Glove, having achieved 16 clean sheets in 32 appearances.

This was a significant milestone in his first season with the Arsenal team, and he has since remained as number one, playing a key role in the club’s pursuit of titles.

While Jääskeläinen said Raya is the best goalkeeper in the Premier League at the moment, he does not think he should be up there with the most experienced.

He believes other goalkeepers have earned an advantage through their consistency over a longer period.

“You have lots of top goalkeepers that I think are still a little bit ahead because of the years they’ve done it in the Premier League.”

“He’s not up there with them yet, but he’s definitely going in the right direction.”

Raya has already proven he can play to the top standard. However, it will be his consistency moving forward that will define his long-term reputation.

Petr Čech, Peter Schmeichel and Edwin van der Sar have all gained great reputations over the years. There is still plenty of time for Raya to do the same, but he will need to keep up his high standards whilst wearing an Arsenal shirt.

At just 31, Raya has a lot left in his career. If he can continue playing at this level, it will make the debate around whether he is one of the best goalkeepers in the Premier League even more intriguing.

#2059. West Ham Winger Faces Another Fitness Test As Parker Waits

West Ham United are facing something of a dilemma over Manor Solomon, with Israel reportedly considering bringing the winger back into the team for Tuesday’s Nations League clash against Austria. The 26-year-old has been struggling with back pain during the international break, although Israel manager Ran Ben-Shimon has suggested that he is feeling better after returning to training.

According to Sport5, Solomon could now be in contention for the Austria fixture. That follows comments from Ben-Shimon suggesting the player had trained and was feeling better, although his fitness would need to be reassessed.

For West Ham manager Scott Parker, however, the situation presents an interesting dilemma. We desperately need the old Manor Solomon back, but I wonder whether another international appearance is really what the player needs right now.

Manor Solomon representing Israel during a difficult international break. The West Ham winger has been carrying a back injury since October

Look, I’m caught between a rock and a hard place on this one.

There is part of me that hopes Solomon gets a game, so he can get back to form. Because there’s no doubt about it, West Ham have a far bigger chance of getting promoted this season if Manor Solomon is back to the sort of form he was in when he played for Leeds in the Championship.

Clearly Scott Parker has faith in him because he has seen how destructive he can be in that division. Getting that player back would be a significant boost for the Hammers, particularly with the Championship promotion race starting to get serious.

But, and it’s a big but, we have had complaints of back injury during the October internationals, and we’ve already had it during this international break.

As I reported earlier in October, a West Ham source confirmed that Solomon was in considerable pain after an exhausting international schedule. More recently, we reported on his latest back problem, which restricted his training with Israel.

There is also part of me that wonders whether we would not have been better served, and the player himself, actually resting up, getting some medical treatment, or even being given ten days off completely to recuperate and joining back up with the squad for the last few days of training before the Preston game.

As things stand, there’s nothing in his recent form that suggests he could start and should start against Preston. And without him being on the training pitch during this international break to impress Scott Parker, he’s not going to be able to catch the manager’s eye.

Edvin Austbø has certainly given Parker something to think about on the left wing. Indeed, Austbø’s recent performances have highlighted the competition for Solomon’s place, and I suspect the Norwegian is currently ahead of him in the pecking order.

But I don’t want to write off Solomon. We know what he’s capable of producing in this division, and West Ham will need every bit of attacking quality available if we’re serious about getting back into the Premier League.

Hopefully, Israel will make the sensible decision and only involve him against Austria if he is genuinely fit enough to play. We cannot afford another international break to leave us with a player who returns to east London carrying another injury.

Israel are bottom of Nations League B Group 3 with 3 points from 5 (0 wins, 3 draws, 2 defeats, goal difference -5) and Austria top it unbeaten on 11. On those numbers it looks more like a damage-limitation night than one to rebuild form in. (Figures from the PickGround match page, posting as the PickGround team, free prediction site.) #2058. Mikel Merino speaks out on Arsenal role as January rumours grow

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Merino has played down any speculation around his potential exit during January. He has made it clear he will fight to keep his place in the squad.

He also spoke about speculation around Martin Zubimendi, who has been linked with Atletico Madrid.

Both players have been questioned over their role in Mikel Arteta’s midfield. There is increasing competition for positions at the Emirates.

Merino feels that there is always an intense fight for places in top-level football. He spoke about how strong and deep Arsenal are ahead of their Nations League clash against the Czech Republic.

Both Merino and Zubimendi were also keen to stress that they will be looking to gain more opportunities and not get distracted.

Merino has had to adapt to his new role at Arsenal after playing a key role on his team’s side. However, Merino has struggled to get into the starting XI.

He admitted that he wants to play more games. However, he acknowledged that it was difficult to get into the team, as there are so many quality players.

This shows that his playing time has not affected his relationship with Arteta.

Merino was able to understand what it is like to be a manager because his father was a football coach. He understands that tough decisions have to be made when selecting your starting XI.

This can be an important aspect in a difficult season, as all players need to play their part to make sure Arsenal stay challenging in multiple competitions.

Merino’s comments are very interesting considering the uncertainty around Zubimendi.

There are reports Zubimendi has been linked to Atletico Madrid and whether he will be looking to move in January.

Merino didn’t give a definite answer that these players will stay at Arsenal, but the comments do suggest they are looking to improve their position in the squad.

He also underlined the importance of having good cover, with Spain boss Luis de la Fuente believing players coming off the bench can be just as vital as those starting.

This is something Arsenal will want their squad to take on board. They will not always be able to play the same midfielders due to the demands of playing in both domestic and European competitions.

There is still time for Merino and Zubimendi to turn their situation around, so what they are doing now may not indicate how important they are going to be in the campaign.

Arsenal will be keen to keep Merino happy, and he has made his position clear. This should give some reassurance ahead of the January transfer window.

#2057. Defender David Bates recalled by Scotland after almost eight-year absence

Published: 13:34 EST, 11 November 2026 | Updated: 13:45 EST, 11 November 2026

David Bates has been rewarded for a strong start to life in Turkey by earning a recall to the Scotland squad almost eight years after his last appearance for the national team.

The 30-year-old centre-back has been added to Sebastien Pocognoli’s group after Luis Binks withdrew ahead of the Nations League matches against North Macedonia on Friday and Switzerland on Monday.

Bates currently plays for Amedspor and has started all 11 matches this season as his club have soared to third in the Turkish Super Lig.

The defender won all four of his caps under Alex McLeish while playing for Hamburg in the 2018/19 campaign. His last appearance for the national team was in a 2-0 win over San Marino in a Euro 2020 qualifier in March 2019, in what also turned out to be McLeish’s last game in charge.

Former Rangers player Bates joined Amedspor in the summer after spending four years in Belgium with Mechelen and then Standard Liege following his Aberdeen exit in 2022.

Brondby captain Binks is the second centre-back to drop out of the squad initially named by Pocognoli last week, after Swansea’s Stephen Welsh sustained an injury and had to be replaced by Los Angeles FC’s Ryan Porteous last weekend.

David Bates playing for Scotland against Albania back in 2018

Sebastian Pocognoli is keen to create a winning culture in the Scotland squad

The head coach is viewing this month’s double-header as crucial to his preparations for the Euro 2028 qualification campaign, which could begin in March if Scotland are not involved in Nations League play-offs.

'I think that these two games are, if we start the qualification in March, the last package of games that we have to use to be ready for the March camp,' Pocognoli said.

'If it’s not the case (that the Euro campaign starts in March), we can play friendly games which can be useful for June, so we have more time to prepare. And maybe, if we are really strong in the next two games, maybe we have play-off games. That’s the ambition.

'These can be useful also for the process of the qualification. But if the qualification starts (in March), we need to be ready. That’s why this camp for me is crucial in terms of demanding to the players, try to impress me and do everything to improve.

'I believe if we want to go to the Euros and be successful, we need to build this qualification feeling by winning games and to build the best winning culture possible.'

#2056. Arsenal Women vs Barcelona: Arsenal show their resilience with impressive draw against Barcelona

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal Women vs Barcelona: Arsenal Women claimed a vital point at the Camp Nou. They drew 1-1 against Barcelona in the Champions League on Thursday night.

Frida Maanum gave the Gunners the lead in the first half. Caroline Graham Hansen scored to bring the game level after the break. Whilst Renee Slegers will be disappointed to miss out on all three points, her side played some encouraging football.

Despite needing to play differently, Arsenal had an even game against an opposition who would inevitably have long periods of the ball. The visitors played very well defensively and looked dangerous when they could attack.

The result showed that Slegers has a team that can adapt to new challenges. This could be important for Arsenal as they try to make a mark in Europe.

One of the key decisions made by Slegers was to include Maanum. She had only recently returned from injury but made an impressive impact against Barcelona.

The midfielder scored early on to start the game in the right direction. Slegers was very impressed with her play when not in possession. She was praised for her communication skills and assisting others.

Arsenal’s attacking play was mainly based on fast counter attacks. They knew they wouldn’t be able to play in the same style as Barcelona and therefore tried to take advantage of the space on offer.

However, there were chances created in both halves. Arsenal may have been more clinical with their chances, they were always a threat.

Eventually, Barcelona scored their equaliser through Graham Hansen. Slegers said that Arsenal lost their shape during the move, which gave Hansen too much space.

It was a reminder of how quickly Barcelona can strike if you lose your concentration for a moment.

The Arsenal manager was pleased with the way her players implemented the game plan, showing tenacity, discipline, good communication and bravery.

Slegers admitted Arsenal did lose the ball too much in the second half, and by keeping it longer, they would have taken the pressure off defending against Barca’s dangerous attack.

There was some frustration that Arsenal couldn’t hold onto their lead, and there were more chances created towards the end of the game. The visitors got into good positions to win corners but couldn’t get the winning goal.

They will feel very positive from their display. They put themselves up against one of the biggest tests in the Camp Nou and proved that they can cause Barcelona problems.

There isn’t much time to really reflect on the result ahead of their game against the London City Lionesses on Sunday, but Slegers now has another example of what her side can do when they play with purpose and structure.

Alan Shearer believes Ezri Konsa has been the Premier League’s best player this season after an impressive start to his Arsenal career.

Konsa’s arrival at Emirates has provided Mikel Arteta with a defender, but his performances have caused some issues for the Arsenal boss. Konsa is now a viable option for Arteta to play, as Saliba is fit again.

Shearer feels Konsa has done enough to be regarded as the best player in the division so far.

He stated in the Metro that Konsa has been effective while playing for Arsenal in the defence.

The former Newcastle player believed that Konsa has been the best player in the Premier League during this season. He pointed out the proof of the performance in the defence, arguing that people did not notice the missing Saliba until now.

Konsa has proved just how much he has grown since moving to the Emirates. Arsenal have been very strong at the back with Konsa playing a massive part.

But there is a problem with Konsa’s performances. Even if Saliba is fit he has done more than enough to make his place difficult to take.

Saliba is still one of Arsenal’s most important defenders, so Arteta will be delighted to have him back. However, he can’t ignore what Konsa has done in his absence.

It would be a tough call to leave the Englishman out, as he has been in such great form. But it means an experienced first choice defender would be left on the bench.

Konsa and Saliba could make a good partnership if they are given time to understand each other on the pitch. They will need to play regularly to do this.

Konsa has made it very hard not to pick him. He has been performing so well that he has created a dilemma for the manager.

Shearer highlights how well he has done by saying this. Arteta will have to figure out how to make the most out of his two quality defenders.

#2052. Andy Burnham and his comments on the 3pm Saturday blackout got me thinking

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

The headline declared that Andy Burnham has urged caution on scrapping the 3pm Saturday TV blackout.

Ahead of this latest international break I was at the Fulham v Newcastle United game, the novelty of it being a rare 3pm kick-off for NUFC.

The BBC article that I was reading, stated: “The blackout originated in the 1960s when Burnley chairman Bob Lord encouraged the EFL to oppose television coverage of 3pm Saturday matches.”

I understand the sentiments expressed by Andy Burnham, as he doesn’t want to be seen as upsetting the lower league clubs who are dependent on match day receipts and the peripheral spending.

However, when you look at the amount of games in the Premier League played outside the Saturday 3pm kick-off, you begin to wonder.

The working man’s game where you could rock up to St James’ Park with a belly full of ale for a 3pm kick-off and pay at the turnstiles are a thing of the past and long gone.

Take a look at the Newcastle United fixtures so far this season and you will see Fulham really was novelty.

In these first five months of the 2026-27 Premier League season only three 3pm Saturday kick-offs for Newcastle United, home to Hull and away to Fulham, plus Ipswich away next month.

All of these Premier League games played outside of the 3pm Saturday slot is a boon for lower league clubs. Due to technology, I can watch Newcastle play at 3pm on a Saturday even when I don’t get a ticket for the match.

But the 3pm Saturday kick-off is such a rarity it has become an irrelevance.

As the Newcastle United games are now shown at an increasingly ridiculous range of times, that frees up Saturday afternoon for me and my mates to go down to Dulwich Hamlet on a Saturday afternoon and have a pint of Peckham pale ale on the terracing while watching live football in the Isthmian Premier League.

So as me and my lad fail to get home tickets for Newcastle United on a regular basis now (no need for tiny violins), we are down the Hamlet on a Saturday afternoon with hundreds of other like minded Premier League team supporting people, who simply want to see live football kicking off at a proper time.

#2051. Why England fans booed Arsenal defender Ben White again after Croatia win

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Ben White returned to the England senior squad on Thursday night but received a hostile reception at Wembley.

The Arsenal defender came on as a substitute in the 78th minute of England’s 2-1 Nations League win against Croatia but was met with boos from a section of the crowd.

Thomas Tuchel has since defended White, stating that he should be in the squad. However, this raises questions as to why some England supporters have turned on the defender after he has continued to perform well for Arsenal.

The answer lies with the 2022 World Cup in Qatar.

He did not play in the first two group games for England and missed the last group game against Wales due to illness.

There were reports that White had fallen out with assistant manager Steve Holland, but neither the FA nor England management confirmed this was the reason he left.

Gareth Southgate later stated there were no problems between White and Holland and that White had simply withdrawn himself from selection after the tournament.

This meant that White would never play for England again while Southgate was still manager. Some fans were clearly upset by this, as they felt White was shunning his country.

When White returned under Tuchel in March 2026, he was met with a negative reaction. He was booed by the crowd when he scored his first-ever international goal against Uruguay. White was also booed in the same fashion against Japan.

He has since explained that supporters might view the situation differently if they knew the full story. However, he has chosen not to reveal the private details behind his departure.

Tuchel has shown little concern about White being affected by the controversy.

The England boss said after the win on Thursday that he had not heard any boos and praised White’s qualities, work ethic and desire to play for his country.

He believes White can help England win games and added that he has the backing of the coaching staff.

Arsenal fans will be unsurprised to learn that White is still a valuable asset for Mikel Arteta, offering defensive consistency and being able to play as a right or centre-back.

The bad press that White is receiving from England fans will not go away any time soon. However, he has not done anything to hurt his reputation at club level. The reasons why he left for the World Cup are personal.

The main focus for Arsenal will be getting him match fit again. As for the England fans, we shall see.

#2049. Arsenal Women vs London City Lionesses preview: Gunners face tough test at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal look to build on their recent win over Liverpool as they return to the Emirates Stadium but will face an impressive London City Lionesses side who are a potent attacking threat on their travels.

Renée Slegers’ team have struggled to convert their strong performances into points in the league this season. They have lost their last four WSL games at home, however, becoming the first team in WSL history to draw four successive games at home.

Their bad run must come to an end. They have taken just three points from their first nine league games so far this season, scoring a miserable total of 10 goals. However, they look like they have more in the tank and will need to be much more clinical in front of the goal.

The day also marks Slegers’ 50th game in charge of the WSL. The Dutch manager has only lost six league games so far this season, with only Chelsea having a better record from teams that have been ever-present in the division.

Arsenal can’t take their opposition lightly after London City’s impressive recent form. They have won two and drawn once in their last three away league games, scoring 12 goals.

Eder Maestre’s side has become a potent attacking force. Daniëlle van de Donk has provided four assists this season, and Delphine Cascarino has scored three goals and provided two assists.

Kadidiatou Diani can be dangerous. She has scored or assisted in all four of her league games away from home this season.

London City’s attacking prowess means they are a far different proposition to some teams Arsenal have faced at home. They have been in a positive situation for 62.7 per cent of their league minutes this season, the highest in the WSL.

Arsenal will have to deal with the attacking threats but look to utilise their ball possession better. Giving London City an opportunity to counter could make an already tough afternoon even tougher.

Alessia Russo comes into the game having found the net in her last three WSL games and will be key in converting Arsenal’s chances into goals.

The Gunners also showed their fighting spirit in their midweek clash against Barcelona. They drew 1-1 at Camp Nou to become the first side to take points off the Spanish giants in the Women’s Champions League at home since April 2024.

Slegers’ team will feel emboldened by that result but will have to change their tactics against London City.

They have won all seven games against Arsenal so far, scoring an impressive 22 goals and only conceding once. However, given London City’s recent away form, they will be hotly favoured.

They certainly have the quality to take all three points, but they must finally convert their dominance into a comfortable home win.

#2048. Arsenal transfer interest in Alphadjo Cisse: Arsenal face a £50m decision as AC Milan youngster attracts Premier League interest

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal transfer interest in Alphadjo Cisse is starting to take shape. Arsenal are monitoring the development of AC Milan starlet Alphadjo Cissé, although his sensational start to the season could prove to be costly for Mikel Arteta.

The 20-year-old has made a blistering start to his Serie A career and attracted interest from a number of Premier League clubs after his impressive displays so far. Arsenal are reported to be interested.

The Gunners are among several clubs, including Brighton, keeping tabs on the player, according to Italian football news website Tuttomercatoweb. The striker is rumoured to be worth up to 60 million euros. That’s an eye-watering sum for a player who is just starting out in his professional career.

Cissé was signed by Milan from Hellas Verona in February 2026 but was allowed to finish the season on loan at Catanzaro.

Prior to joining the San Siro, he had already showcased his potential with six goals in 22 games in Serie B. He has made a huge impact since moving up to the top division.

Cissé has found the net five times in nine Serie A games this season. He has also chipped in with three goals in four Europa League games, making a total of eight goals in 13 games.

This has made him an important addition to the Milan squad. He is also versatile, as he can play as an attacking midfielder or higher up the park.

Arsenal may find his versatility attractive. Arteta likes players who can play in different positions and with injuries, and with a busy fixture list, he will want to have players who can do that.

The main issue could be that Milan are keen to keep their young star.

It is reported that the Italian club have turned down a verbal offer from Brighton and are looking to extend Cissé’s contract, which currently runs until June 2030.

This indicates that Milan is considering his future, rather than looking to make a profit. It also demonstrates just how quickly his reputation has developed.

Arsenal have the funds to target exciting young talent, but would they want to spend so much on Cissé?

There is no indication that he would be an instant replacement for Arsenal’s current attacking options. However, Cissé’s age, goal-scoring ability and versatility make him an interesting prospect.

This could be one to keep an eye on beyond January. Milan have little motivation to sell, and Arsenal might want to see how he develops before committing themselves.

Cissé has already hit the ground running in Italy and now needs to prove that his initial success can turn into something more consistent.

#2047. Incredible story as Newcastle United teenager creates history with Brazil

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Newcastle United teenager Matheos Ferreira has made history in the colours of Brazil.

The exciting 18-year-old winger got his first ever call up for his country this month.

Matheos Ferreira named in the Brazil Under 20s squad for a double header with Peru.

He was an unused sub on Friday when the young Brazil stars won 2-0.

Then on Monday he made history, Matheos Ferreira becoming the first player developed at the Newcastle United Academy to go on to represent Brazil at international level.

The 18-year-old is from Berwick but is eligible to represent the country through his Brazilian father, Thiago.

Not only did Matheos Ferreira make his first ever start for the Brazil Under 20s on Monday, it only took him 14 minutes to score!

The United winger putting Brazil into the lead against Peru Under 20s in Belem.

— Bradley Robson (@bradrobson2605) November 17, 2026

Matheos Ferreira seeing his dream international debut get even better when he also supplied an assist as the Brazil youngsters routed the opposition and won 9-0 against Peru.

The Newcastle United Academy star talked to official NUFC club media from Brazil ahead of his debut: “I had to start training as soon as I got there but I think it’s shown how far I have come as a player because I wasn’t too nervous or anxious… I am doing the thing I love the most, playing football, and it was nice to get on the pitch and train with the squad. It’s been busy! The culture is completely different here but I really like it. We’re having to train in the evening as it is usually over 30 degrees during the day. Since the first training session, I’ve felt very welcome by the lads and the coaches as well.”

Matheos Ferreira also qualifies to play potentially for England and Italy (his mam’s Italian).

The winger joined the Newcastle United Academy in 2020 after playing for Berwick Juniors and Cramlington Juniors and recalled the moment he found out he’d got his first Brazil call up: “My first reaction was just pure joy. I was so happy. My parents wanted to tell me first before I checked my phone but it was still a great moment to share with them, who were both very emotional. I think it’s a reward for how I’ve been playing but also how I’ve managed to conduct myself off the pitch with my recovery, willingness to learn and reflecting on how I can improve on the matches I’ve played in. It’s an amazing achievement but I don’t want this to be the last. I will keep pushing on and try to get that next call-up or progress into the first-team. Once I reach one level, it’s about getting to the next one. It doesn’t stop there. We’ve got the South American (Youth Football) Championship in January and the Under-20 World Cup is next year so I’m pushing really hard to try and get into both squads. I’m doing everything I can to try and make my international debut. It would mean a lot. I haven’t got this big pressure of needing to do well – I just want to get on that pitch, show what I can do and do what I love the most.”

Matheos Ferreira remembering: “My first memory of Brazilian football would be my dad showing videos of Ronaldinho, R9 and Neymar,” he added. “The first game I watched live was at Wembley between England and Brazil and Neymar played. I think it finished goalless though! The interest they showed me and the project they have was a big reason [to accept the call-up]. Brazil is a big country so to be recognised by them is amazing. It’s a big opportunity and an honour to represent them.”

The 18-year-old rising Newcastle United star is buzzing with how the 2026-27 season has kicked off, for club and country: “I’ve really enjoyed the start to this season. The coaches trust me as I’ve been playing in different positions for the Under-21s; wing-back, as a winger or a number ten. I think they know what I can provide for the team. I think its shows the qualities I have. Playing as a right wing-back shows I can defend, put my body on the line and then it’s about showing my strengths going forward. I’ve embraced the challenges of switching positions, thinking about the tactical side of the game.”

A brilliant moment on Monday for Matheos Ferreira and here’s to many more appearances (and goals!) in the colours of Brazil AND Newcastle United.

Fellow academy Scot Josh Landers is at Ayr United on loan

#2045. Max Dowman breaks Jude Bellingham record as Arsenal teenager makes England history

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Max Dowman has added another incredible achievement to his burgeoning career after becoming the youngest player to play for the England Under-21s.

The Arsenal youngster was introduced from the bench in Friday’s 1-0 friendly loss to Portugal at Ashton Gate. The 16-year-10-month-13-day-old player is over three months younger than Jude Bellingham was when he played his first game for the Under-21s.

Dowman replaced former Arsenal academy player Amario Cozier-Duberry in the 61st minute. He came on alongside debutant Trey Nyoni as England tried to shift the momentum of a challenging game.

An early strike from Afonso Moreira gave Portugal the lead in the 28th minute. An ineffective England team failed to respond. However, the replacements gave Lee Carsley’s team some late momentum.

Despite the hosts’ attempts to level the score, they failed to break down the Portuguese defence. The result means England have lost for the second game running. However, Dowman had much to be pleased with personally.

Dowman has added to his growing collection of records.

He became the youngest player to play in the Champions League, the youngest goalscorer in the history of the Premier League and the youngest to win the Premier League title.

Breaking Bellingham’s England Under-21 record is another significant step. It shows how quickly Dowman has progressed through the international age groups.

He has already made a big impression at Arsenal with Mikel Arteta’s love of giving younger players a chance, but Dowman will have some stiff competition for first team minutes.

Another positive is his progression on an international scale. He will improve both physically and tactically playing against older players and gain experience at a higher level.

England will host France in their next friendly on Monday, 16th November. Dowman will be hoping to feature after his instant impact on the record books.

Despite the disappointing result against Portugal, it was a landmark evening for the Arsenal players.

Dowman is already building a legacy at club level, but now he can add to his list of records by becoming an England Under-21 record holder.

At just 16, he has plenty of time to improve and show consistent performances to get more opportunities with the senior Arsenal squad.

The Mag Podcast Episode 17: Newcastle United’s 2015-16 relegation with lessons from the past

Published: 16/11/2026 | Duration: 46 mins | Hosts: Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill & David Hutchinson

In Episode 17 of The Mag Pod, we find Jamie, Simon, and Hutch discussing relegation, again! Special focus is on the 2015-16 season when the combined talents of Mike Ashley and Steve McClaren led to the club’s most recent drop to the Championship. So how does the current 2026-27 NUFC situation compare…?

You can play episode 17 on the audio player above or use your favourite podcast platforms including Spotify, Apple Podcasts, Amazon Music, YouTube, and Acast.

Listen to the latest episode of The Mag Pod! Join Jamie, Simon, and Hutch for unfiltered football debate.

Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill and David Hutchinson revisit Newcastle United’s 2015-16 relegation season under the ownership of Mike Ashley, examining the failures under Steve McClaren, the arrival of Rafa Benítez and whether there are lessons to be learned amid the concerns surrounding the current 2026-27 campaign.

The lads also discuss Newcastle United Women’s derby draw with Sunderland and the controversial travel restrictions for the upcoming Tyne-Wear derby.

In the second of three episodes this week, Hutch takes us through another particularly forgettable chapter in Newcastle United’s history. From a disappointing start to the season and a recruitment policy that prioritised potential over experience, to a divided dressing room and a lack of leadership on the pitch, there was no shortage of contributing factors to United’s downfall.

There’s a look back at the performances of Georginio Wijnaldum, Aleksandar Mitrović, Fabricio Coloccini and Moussa Sissoko, as well as some personal memories of the campaign, including a frustrating afternoon at Old Trafford and a particularly miserable pre-Christmas encounter with Aston Villa.

Could an earlier appointment of Rafa Benítez have saved Newcastle from relegation?

The lads discuss the importance of knowing when to make a managerial change, whilst considering the differences between the Newcastle United of a decade ago and the club today.

There’s also an assessment of the increasingly congested Premier League table, the importance of experienced players in a transitional squad and the heroes and villains of the 2015-16 season.

Naturally, a controversial Sergio Agüero goal brings about another argument between Jamie and Simon over the merits of VAR, with all the maturity listeners have come to expect.

Another nostalgic look at a period Newcastle United fans would rather forget, but one which offers plenty of talking points given the challenges facing United today.

#2043. Prince William asks Aston Villa boss Unai Emery about the team EVERY DAY - as he reveals he's become a bigger supporter since becoming a father to football-mad George

Published: 07:23 EST, 11 November 2026 | Updated: 14:24 EST, 11 November 2026

Aston Villa boss Unai Emery has revealed how passionate Prince William is about his beloved football club and opened up on their relationship.

The Prince of Wales, 44, has been a fan of Villa since his days at Eton when he idolised club legend Paul McGrath - shunning the more conventional Premier League big beasts supported by his schoolmates.

He regularly attends matches at Villa Park and across Europe alongside his son George, and he has been a prominent figure during their success under Emery in recent times.

This included the Prince partying with Villa's squad after last season's Europa League triumph and he watched them against Barcelona in the Champions League at the Nou Camp last week.

And, in a special edition of Country Life magazine, which was guest edited by the 44-year-old, Emery reflected on their relationship and how often they talk about the club.

’During the transfer window, he’s asking about the team every day,' the Villa boss said. 'How is the team? How are you? How are we going to play some players? How are we going to do? He is always so close to us and we appreciate it so, so much.

Unai Emery has revealed how passionate Prince William is about Aston Villa and opened up on their relationship

The Prince pictured alongside his son George at Villa's game against Barcelona last week

A special edition of Country Life, guest edited by the 44-year-old, featured a piece on Emery

’When he’s with us, he’s a fantastic guy. We’ve had lunch together, he’s watched training sessions, we share conversations, everything about football. He shows his humble personality with us - I can’t forget it. For me, it seems unbelievable.

'He’s our boss, but he’s always trying to be at our level, respecting so much everything we are doing here. If he’s sitting with us to have lunch, he asks “Where am I sitting?” Wherever you want! It’s fantastic.

‘We are scoring, he is celebrating, it is fantastic for us. If we can give him good news, good results, good moments in football, we are proud of it.’

In the same article, the Prince of Wales went on to reflect on his love of football and Villa, revealing how he has become increasingly passionate over the years.

He added: 'Football feels like a second family to me, I love the tribal nature of being among others who support the same team. We all experience the highs and lows of following that team together and you get the feeling that you belong somewhere.

'I used to be a defender, usually right back, and I’m hoping to play in a few fathers’ five-a-side matches. Since having children, I’ve become even more fanatical than I thought I would be about the game.

'I wasn’t like this when I was 18 or 20, I kept an eye from a distance. Now, I probably think and talk about Aston Villa every day. I enjoy looking to see what is going on: the stats, the players’ recovery, tactics and matches.'

The Prince is regularly pictured watching his beloved team at Villa Park and across Europe

When asked previously about why he settled on the Midlands club, Prince William shared that it was the thrill of supporting a club without season after season of successes which had brought him to Villa.

'A long time ago at school I got into football big time. I was looking around for clubs. All my friends were either Man United fans or Chelsea fans and I didn’t want to follow the run-of-the-mill teams,' he told the BBC in 2015.

'I wanted to have a team that was more mid-table that could give me more emotional rollercoaster moments.

'Aston Villa’s always had a great history. I have got friends of mine who support Aston Villa and one of the first FA Cup games I went to was Bolton v Aston Villa back in 2000. Sadly, Villa went on to lose to Chelsea (in the final).

'It was fantastic, I sat with all the fans with my red beanie on, and I was with all the Brummie fans and had a great time.

'It was the atmosphere, the camaraderie and I really felt that there was something I could connect with.'

#2042. Martin Ødegaard sends clear message to Arsenal amid Bayern Munich transfer rumours

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Martin Ødegaard has spoken out about the speculation surrounding his future. He has stated he would like to stay at Arsenal for the future.

The captain has also admitted that talks about a new deal have not yet begun. Ødegaard is currently under contract until the summer of 2028. This leaves Arsenal with an important decision to make over the coming months.

Bayern Munich are being linked with an interest in the midfielder according to reports from Germany. The Bundesliga giants are said to be keeping their eyes on the player’s situation.

Speaking while on international duty with Norway, Ødegaard was quick to dismiss the speculation. He made it clear he is settled in north London and sees his future being at the Emirates Stadium.

There is no suggestion that the 27-year-old is seeking a move. Although comments do highlight a contract issue that Arsenal will be keen to resolve in the short term.

The Norwegian’s laid-back attitude should be encouraging for supporters.

He has made it clear that he is happy at the club and wants to stay here for the time being. This means that there is no need to panic.

However, Arsenal will have to get him to sign on the dotted line. Leaving his captain in the last year of his contract with no progress would cause an unnecessary distraction.

Since his permanent move from Real Madrid in 2021, Ødegaard has become a key player in Mikel Arteta’s plans. He has grown into a team leader and is one of the most influential players in the dressing room.

His importance has been shown by his inclusion in all 10 of Arsenal’s Premier League games so far this season. He has scored three goals.

This is a big improvement on last season, where he was hampered by injuries and failed to make much of an impact in front of goal.

It wouldn’t be a shock to see Bayern Munich show interest in Ødegaard, but don’t take this as a sign he is close to a move.

There is little reason for Arsenal to consider losing their captain. He brings so much more to the team than just his technical ability and has been relied upon by Arteta to lead by example.

The midfielder was also pivotal in the 1-0 win over Hull City on 7 November, scoring the winner in the 72nd minute to give the league leaders three crucial points.

Arsenal have put themselves in a good position in the title race. Keeping their key players happy will be important in the second half of the campaign.

The Gunners will be focused on their game against Newcastle United on 21st November.

Arsenal fans have no real reason to panic at the moment, with the captain wanting to stay at the club and his comments suggesting he is happy.

However, it would be a good idea for the club to try and ensure his long-term future at the club. Ødegaard is still an important part of Arteta’s strategy, and ensuring he stays at the club would be a smart move.

#2041. GdS: A midfielder with a handbrake – Sylla’s journey to USG and why Milan are interested

AC Milan have been considering a move for Massire Sylla recently and the Senegalese defender’s stock is only rising currently.

There are plenty of defensive options for Milan in the winter mercato. Goncalo Inacio is probably the preference for most, but the profile of Sylla cannot be ignored. Another signing for the future, he’s similar in many ways to Sankhoun Diawara, and the Frenchman has already started to have an impact.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Gazzetta dello Sport have looked a little at his background and his profile. A physical defender, he has previously admitted that he’s a lover of ‘the fight’; a player who loves duels and wins them a lot of the time.

Called up to the Senegalese national team this month, his story is one of patience, tenacity and the ability to persist when faced with obstacles. His career started at Diambars Academy, one of the most notorious in Senegal, starting as a midfielder but eventually moving back to defence.

His coaches wanted him to learn to play with his feet, but his defensive qualities shone, hence the move back. With that, though, he started to see the ball more and both aspects of his game improved substantially.

From there, he joined Pau in 2024 and within six months, he was playing for the senior team in Ligue 2. At 19, he moved to Lyn and then Union Saint-Gilloise paid €2 million for him last January.

It wasn’t an immediate fit, though, and he was called overexcited, thanks to his yellow card record. Yet, he’s settled and now is a key figure in a three-man defence and picking out his best traits are difficult because there are so many.
